**Chaining falls out for free (PQ-5).** An interior graph addresses its own nodes' ports exactly as a room does, so a bag's interior edge from port 4 may target `ROPE:2`. No extra machinery.

#### What a port number is not (added 2026-08-06)

**Written because this section has now generated two misreadings of the same family, each of which propagated into assessments before it was caught.** The section says clearly what a port *is*; it said nothing about what a port number is **not**, and the negative turns out to be the load-bearing half.

A port number is a **boundary slot on the host object**, allocated by that host, meaningful only as one end of one crossing. It is **not**:

- **A name for the interior node behind it.** `OBJECT#ROPE:1` does not identify a part. The part is an ordinary nominal id --- `OBJECT#RopeEnd1` --- and the port merely has an edge to it. The two worked examples in this section are already symmetric on this point: `port 1 -[TiedTo]-> OBJECT#RopeEnd1` and `port 1 -[SolderedTo]-> OBJECT#BatteryCase`.
- **A reusable public interface.** One port records **one** crossing; two connections to the same host are two ports. See [Ports are single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).
- **A fan-out point.** A port has exactly one interior edge and one exterior referrer.
- **Evidence about the interior at all.** Numbering is a property of the *boundary*; whether the interior identifies its parts positionally or nominally is a separate question (**PQ-9**), which the port answers neither way.

**The error this last bullet prevents actually happened.** The retired **AB-16** briefly held that extent parts are identified by *where* and containment parts by *what*, citing `ROPE:1` --- reading the boundary slot as the part's name. It was the last surviving argument for that row, and it was an artifact of this section.

#### A port is a scale boundary, not a relay (2026-08-06)

**The two edges joined by a port need not carry the same kind, and usually will not.** This reverses the leaning recorded on **PQ-1 (original)** and changes what a port is understood to be.

**The rope made matching kinds look necessary, and that was an artifact.** `boulder -TiedTo-> ROPE:1` and `port 1 -TiedTo-> RopeEnd1` agree because a rope end is *the same kind of thing* as a rope --- homogeneous extent, where a part is a smaller instance of the whole. Under homogeneity a port looks like an identity function, quietly relaying one relation inward.

**A heterogeneous case breaks it immediately.** A power cord run into a flashlight: the exterior edge is `PowerCord -[ThreadsInto]-> OBJECT#FlashLight:1`, and the interior edge is `port 1 -[SolderedTo]-> OBJECT#BatteryCase`. Both are true, neither is a copy, and they describe the connection at the level each graph operates on. **They are two relations at different scales, joined by the port** --- so a port is a **scale boundary at which the description changes**, not a relay.

**Consequence for storage: each side stores its own kind, and nothing is duplicated.** The earlier leaning ("the kind lives inside only") rested on avoiding a two-writer problem across two shards. **That premise is gone** --- if the kinds legitimately differ there is no shared fact to disagree about, and the consistency worry evaporates for the same reason the ingress list's did: complementary, not redundant. **No new mechanism is needed either:** the exterior edge already carries `HostRelationalEdgeKind`, and the interior edge is an ordinary edge in the flashlight's graph.

**Consequence for coarsening, and it is better than tolerable --- it is lossless at the level being read.** Under matching kinds, coarsening felt like degradation: *tied to the rope's end* -> *tied to the rope*. Under heterogeneous kinds there is no degradation at all: `PowerCord -ThreadsInto-> FLASHLIGHT:1` coarsens to `PowerCord -ThreadsInto-> FLASHLIGHT`, which reads **"a power cord threads into the flashlight"** --- unchanged and still correct. **Because the exterior kind was never about the interior part, coarsening loses only the interior fact, which was the detail by construction.**

**Qualified 2026-08-07 --- "lossless at the level being read" was stated without naming the reader, and it is only true for one of them.** Under [the five axes](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to), coarsening is an **apprehension** mechanism: a player told "a power cord threads into the flashlight" has lost nothing. An **LLM** asked whether the strap can be untied, or what the cord is soldered to, has lost precisely the fact it needs --- and reasoning is lossless by design where apprehension is lossy by design. **So coarsening on the reasoning path is a defect, not a graceful degradation.** The likely fix is small and is *not* being adopted here: coarsening stays a read-side rule for **description**, and the reasoning path opens the bag, paying a hop on a path that already expects to pay for detail. Recorded as a defect rather than resolved, since coarsening is calibration on the [not-locked list](#what-is-not-locked-and-must-not-ride-in-on-this) and this is a frame rather than a decision.

**Sharpened later the same day, and the diagnosis moves: "coarsening" names *two* operations, and they have opposite requirements about silence.**

| Operation | Situation | Is there a finer fact? | Must it announce? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Failure recovery** --- the case this section was written for | `OBJECT#BAG:4` where 4 is not a legal port, or no interior edge leaves it | **No.** The address was bad | **No.** There is nothing to announce |
| **Deliberate summarization** --- the case the reasoning axis exposes | Port 4 is valid and the reader chose not to traverse | **Yes** | **Yes** --- otherwise the omission is silent, which is the reasoning axis's one hard requirement |

**So the repair is not "reasoning always opens the bag."** It is that the summarized form must **say that a finer fact exists**, exactly as [a span reports a snag without reporting where](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07). Cheaper than a mandatory hop and it fixes the actual defect, which was silence rather than compression. **The two operations should get different names before either is implemented** --- one word covering a case that must be quiet and a case that must not is how the wrong one gets built.

**No compatibility matrix --- and this is an existing decision applying, not a new one.** The tempting next step is to police coherence across the boundary (is *threads into* compatible with *resting on*?). **Do not.** That is a small version of the compile-fictional-physics-into-deterministic-structure move **AB-19 already rejected**: an abstraction's job is to be the right-sized packet of context for an LLM step, not to replace it with a lookup. Cross-boundary coherence is authoring's and improvisation's responsibility; reasoning about consequences is the LLM step's.

**Two rules that follow.**

1. **The port is the unit of lifecycle, not either edge.** With independent kinds the halves can diverge under dissolution --- sever the exterior edge, leave the interior one, and a port is soldered to a battery case while exiting nowhere. Rather than defining that state, **releasing the port takes both halves.** Consistent with [single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).
2. **Do not "optimize" the rope.** Someone reading rope data will see `TiedTo` on both sides and conclude the kind is stored redundantly. **It is coincidentally equal, not redundant.** Build nothing that assumes matching and nothing that assumes differing.

### The strongest argument for ports is not the traversal one

Direct addressing (`OBJECT#ROPE/OBJECT#RopeEnd1` written into the room's edge) would also satisfy traversal. **What ports add is encapsulation** --- and the payoff lands squarely on the two hardest lifecycle rows, neither of which ports were designed for:

| Row | Under direct addressing | Under ports |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **AB-17 reabsorption** | Coiling the rope dissolves `RopeEnd1`; every external edge naming it must be found and rewritten across hosts | Rebind port 1 to the root. **Nothing outside the rope changes.** One internal edit |
| **AB-31 fission** | The member set repartitions and external references dangle --- which is the question the row opened with | Ports rebind; external references survive by construction |

**The port is the object's published interface; its parts are the implementation.** Interior refactoring being non-breaking is exactly what those rows were failing to get.

#### A third argument, on a third axis: the port is the summarization seam (2026-08-07)

**Both arguments above are on one axis each --- traversal is [positioning](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to), encapsulation is mutation --- and this section chose between them. It missed a *reasoning* argument that was present in the original sketch and never developed.** The scheme's stated goal was that *looking at the rope alone tells you how its parts connect and where they connect externally*; that was written as a convenience and is worth considerably more than that.

**The mechanism: a relation on an interior part crosses the port and is visible at the whole, unexpanded.** A thread snagged on something in one room --- the snag is a relation on a constituent, it ports up, and **reading the span alone already reports that a snag exists.** Where and how is retrievable and **omittable**. That is the [no-silent-omission](#correction-same-day-reasoning-wants-no-silent-omission-not-losslessness) property delivered structurally: the summary never hides the *existence* of a fact, only its detail.

**Why the third axis matters more than a stronger argument on the first two.** Ports already win positioning and mutation, so another argument there changes no ranking. This is the first developed argument for ports that a **different audience** would make, and under the axis frame that is the kind of evidence that actually moves a decision.

**The honest competitor check, per [the falsification discipline](#what-counts-as-a-falsification-case-learned-the-hard-way-2026-08-05).** A flat representation *can* answer "is there a snag" --- with a reverse index over the constituents, which is **AB-36**. But that is a special-purpose index built to recover what nesting supplies structurally: **a second mechanism for a subclass, which is [position 3](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-clause-1b-actually-costs)** in the precise sense this plan already rejected. The flat form is rescuable only by an epicycle. **Stated rather than asserted**, because "the alternative obviously cannot do this" is the habit that produced two reversed verdicts here.

**Worked example:** [C7's nesting question](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07).

### The root node is required, not incidental

The scheme needs `OBJECT#ROPE` to be a node in its own graph, because `part` edges must emanate from the whole. **That is AB-3's pre-registered trigger firing a third time** --- once from composition (where the trigger was written), once from hosting ([AB-33](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation)), now from ports. At which point `CarryClosureFragment = {rootId, members, edges}` is simply what a ported object's graph looks like.

### Consequence: ports re-price the three positions table

**The "assume multi-host for the moment" framing is not a neutral simplification.** [Position 1](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-clause-1b-actually-costs) was charged with C7's objection: one node cannot record *where along*, so segments are still required, so room B holds both a thread node and a local segment with "pull the thread" ambiguously bound.

**Ports answer that objection without single-hosting.** Room B holds the thread node and a **port reference**; the port names exactly which segment; the where-along record lives in the rope's interior graph where it belongs. And multi-hosting under ports is cheaper than the plan ever priced it --- the rope is *listed* in two rooms but there is exactly **one rope graph**, so the interior is not duplicated. That is closer to a presence index than to real multi-hosting.

**So clause 1b takes a new hit --- not a falsification, but its competitor got materially cheaper.** The narrowed dichotomy stands; the choice *within* it shifts toward the branch that keeps lookup-as-the-clean-rule.

**A second consequence, on AB-25 and AB-26:** sibling links living inside the whole's graph give the part-connection graph a home **below** the rooms rather than above them. AB-26 no longer needs an Area-level carrier, which deflates AB-25 further --- Area was largely being summoned to host things above rooms. See **PQ-8** for the loose end in that claim.

### Open questions (AB-34 sub-questions; ids stable, never reused)

**None of these is settled.** Leanings are recorded where one exists, explicitly as leanings.

| ID | Question | Leaning, if any |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **PQ-1** | **Which graph owns the port record --- and with it, the relation kind?** Widened 2026-08-06 by [single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06): a single-use port is not an indirection, it **is** the relationship, so "where does the kind live" is a special case of "where does the record live." **Answered the same day --- see [The port record](#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06).** Split into **complementary** halves rather than duplicated ones (egress list on the interior graph, ingress port numbers on the exterior node), with the **interior egress list authoritative**. ~~Ingress list accepted as a denormalization inside truth; `positionCache` branch rejected because the room's local read is too hot to depend on cache warmth.~~ **Reversed the same day: the ingress list goes in `positionCache`**, because the room's edges already carry what a coarse read needs, and the list is a **weak** (1:1) denormalization while membership is a **strong** (N:1) one. **PQ-11 retired as a consequence** | **Settled provisionally, after one reversal.** The original sub-question (which side carries the kind) follows from the split; kept below because its reasoning about one-hop resolution is what made the answer acceptable |
| **PQ-1 (original)** | **Where does the relation kind live?** In the sketch, `tied to` appears on **both** the room-side edge and the interior port edge --- same kind, two shards, two writers, and no rule for which wins on disagreement. That is the drift class this codebase already fights (`repairObjectPlacementDrift`, "positionGraph wins"). Splitting one relation across two records is unavoidable, since the endpoints genuinely live in different shards; **the question is which record owns the kind.** Options: (a) outside only, (b) inside only, (c) both, with a stated winner | ~~**Inside only**, to avoid two writers across two shards.~~ **Reversed 2026-08-06 --- see [A port is a scale boundary](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06).** The question assumed the two edges carry **the same** kind, which is an artifact of reasoning from a rope: homogeneous extent makes a part the same kind of thing as its whole, so the port looks like an identity function. **Heterogeneous cases differ legitimately** (`ThreadsInto` outside, `SolderedTo` inside), so **each side stores its own kind and nothing is duplicated** --- complementary facts again, no shared claim to disagree about. What survives from the original reasoning is the one-hop point: room-local description resolves locally, and detail costs a hop |
| **PQ-2** | **Is `present` a distinct edge kind, or a variant of a port?** Mostly redundant --- a part referenced from a room's graph is present there by virtue of the reference --- **except in the case that matters:** a mid-thread segment lying across a corridor floor, attached to nothing, trippable over. [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) in a pass-through room. Presence without attachment is real. **Sharpened 2026-08-06 under [single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) into one record shape with one field varying:** a port's exterior end is *a graph, plus optionally a local endpoint*. **Attachment** = graph `ROOM#A` + endpoint `boulder`. **Presence** = graph `ROOM#A`, no endpoint. Better than "variant" --- it is the same record. **And it reaches further than this row was scoped for: it answers half of AB-6.** If a whole's ports enumerate its presence, presence is **stored, in the whole** --- a carrier neither of that row's two options (stored member set versus derived predicate over edges) contemplated | **The same record, with an optional exterior endpoint.** Flagged for AB-6 rather than settled here |
| **PQ-3** | **Do presence ports collapse when the object is co-located, and re-apply when it spreads?** Proposed as part of the scheme | **Do not collapse in the model; collapse in the projection.** Collapse-and-reapply is a *triggered state transition*, and this plan has repeatedly found triggered transitions are where the cost hides (migration cascade, reabsorption, thrash). If presence ports always exist and merely all point at the same room when coiled, there is no transition to get wrong. The desired collapse --- not listing twenty ports for a coiled rope --- is a render concern, which is the [clean/dirty](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) split already adopted. **Flagged as a leaning with a known counter-view.** **Largely dissolved 2026-08-06 under [single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06): there is nothing structural to collapse.** A coiled rope has one presence port; spread across three rooms it has three. The count tracks reality with no transition to trigger. **The residual redundancy is exactly the [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends) case** --- load-bearing parts persisting while co-located, so N ports all name the same room --- **which is a render concern, where the leaning already put it.** Strengthened rather than changed |
| **PQ-4** | **Port identity and reuse.** Numbered ports invite it: release port 1, mint port 1 again, and a stale external reference silently rebinds to a *different* attachment. **AB-21**'s identity obligation, one layer down | Allocate monotonically and never reuse, **or** key ports by what they bind rather than by ordinal. Not chosen between |
| **PQ-5** | **Do ports chain, and is a port itself addressable as a part?** A rope inside a box, tied to something in the room, needs the box to expose a port forwarding to the rope's port. **This is the recursion question flagged when AB-34 was opened** | **Yes to both** --- which is the fractal working correctly. Consequence: traversal depth becomes unbounded and needs a cycle guard. `computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has(...)` is the precedent, and exists for exactly this reason |
| **PQ-6** | **Egress discovery: how does a host find who references its ports?** Ingress is fine --- a room's edge names the port, which names the host, so plan-time footprint is discoverable in that direction. **The reverse is not indexed anywhere.** See **AB-36**. **Widened 2026-08-06 to absorb the case PQ-7 used to carry:** *interior* mutation invalidating an *exterior* reference. Fission repartitions the rope and releases port 1 while `ROOM#A`'s edge still names it --- and the rope has no idea anyone is pointing at it. **The rule that fixes it is the existing discipline one level over --- a host may not release an externally-referenced port** --- so this is not a new contract, only a new thing that must be discoverable for the old contract to be enforceable. **Largely answered 2026-08-06, and by the design rather than by an index: [ports are single-use and name the graph they exit to](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).** A whole's own graph therefore enumerates its referrers --- **the port list is the reverse index, stored where it is needed** --- so egress discovery is local, the footprint is computable at plan time (which `computeStepSequenceFootprint` running once demands), and the release rule becomes locally enforceable. **What remains open** is whether a port's exterior half is discoverable when the *exterior* graph is the one being mutated without opening the interior, which is PQ-1's record-ownership question | **No longer the blocking item.** Deflated by the single-use design; residue folds into PQ-1 |
| **PQ-7** | **~~Does assert-and-throw survive cross-host references?~~ Restated 2026-08-06 --- the original claim was wrong, and the correction moves the cost off this proposal.** As first written: ports break the same-host locality that makes `RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError` enforceable, so this was "a real loss of a shipped guarantee." **Withdrawn under a worked scenario.** A rope tied to a cliff-top boulder puts the boulder, the rope node and the edge `boulder -TiedTo-> OBJECT#ROPE:1` **all in `ROOM#CliffTop`'s graph** --- so `assertNoRelationalEdgesReferencing` scans one graph and finds it. Requiring every connection to `OBJECT#ROPE`, port-qualified or not, to be dissolved before removal **is** the existing contract, unchanged. **The real question, and it is not about ports:** *when* does a port reference live on a graph the whole's node is absent from? Under **multi-host presence + ports**, never --- the node is in every room it is present in, so anything that can see a port also holds the node, and locality is preserved. Under **single-host at an Area + ports**, always --- room B references the port while the node sits at the Area. **So the exposure belongs to [clause 4](#h1-is-a-conjunction-and-its-clauses-are-tested-separately), not to P3, and ports are merely what made it visible** | **Restated, not open.** Kept rather than deleted per this plan's rule on recording supersessions; the surviving cost is logged against clause 4 |
| **PQ-11** | **RETIRED 2026-08-06, same day it was opened --- the premise was withdrawn.** The ingress list moved from `positionGraph` to `positionCache`, so there is no denormalization in truth, no drift, and no repair story owed. **Kept rather than deleted** per this plan's supersession rule, and because one observation in it survives as a note on **AB-35**: a derived list is repaired by **recompute rather than merge**, which is materially cheaper than `repairObjectPlacementDrift`'s job and is a property worth having on record for whatever the cache's invalidation story turns out to be. Original text follows | **Retired --- premise withdrawn** |
| **PQ-11 (original)** | **The ingress list can drift --- what is the conflict rule, and what repairs it?** Opened 2026-08-06 as the obligation created by accepting the ingress port list as a denormalization inside `positionGraph` rather than in cache. **The conflict rule is the easy half and follows from authority: the interior egress list wins**, mirroring the shipped "`positionGraph` wins" rule for `Meta` placement. **The repair story is the open half, and nothing does this today.** Sub-questions: what detects divergence (a sweep like `repairObjectPlacementDrift`, a read-time reconciliation, or a write-time assertion), what the failure looks like from a player's seat (a port listed on a node that the object no longer exits to renders as... nothing? a coarsened attachment? --- note **the coarsening default makes the *reverse* direction benign** but says nothing about a *phantom* ingress entry), and whether repair is delete-only like the objects lane's precedent or must reconstruct. **Note the asymmetry worth exploiting:** the ingress list is fully derivable from the egress lists of objects present, so repair is a **recompute**, not a merge --- which is a materially easier repair than `repairObjectPlacementDrift`'s. | **Conflict rule settled** (interior wins); recompute-not-merge is the shape; detection and cadence open. Ties to **AB-35** |
| **PQ-12** | **Must relocation follow adjacency, and is that a rule or the caller's business?** Opened 2026-08-06 from [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in). Hauling a rope in moves the far end `ROOM#C -> ROOM#B`, not `ROOM#C -> ROOM#A`, because the rope is continuous and its end travels the path. **Nothing in the port scheme says so.** If it is a **rule** --- a part may relocate only to a host adjacent along the relation licensing its connection to its siblings --- then **AB-26's adjacency licensing appears on the *write* side**, where this plan has only ever discussed it as read-side structure, and that is a genuine widening of that row. If it is **the caller's business**, nothing prevents teleporting a mid-span, and the rope's spatial coherence becomes a convention rather than an invariant. **Cost is not the discriminator:** hauling [Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) twenty rooms is N steps, but the footprint is known up front from the egress list, so `MultiKeyUpdate` folds them into one `transactWrite`. **What is open besides the rule itself:** narration cadence for an N-step haul, which belongs to the relational-narration initiative rather than here | Leaning **rule**, because the invariant is cheap to state and the alternative makes spatial coherence unenforceable --- but this widens AB-26 and should be decided there rather than assumed here |
| **PQ-13** | **Does a plain object carry a presence port?** Opened 2026-08-06 from [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in)'s membership rule --- *an object is present in a room iff at least one of its ports egresses there*. Applied uniformly, a lantern on a table needs one port, egressing to the room, presence-linked to its own root. **The tension is real in both directions.** Uniformity makes a simple object the **degenerate case rather than a special case** --- one port, zero hops --- which is the same shape as the argument that rescued [position 2](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-clause-1b-actually-costs) (*a loop that sometimes iterates once is one mechanism, not two*) and is what the [fractal](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) demands if it is to be honest. ~~**Against it:** this is overhead on the commonest object in the game, on the hottest read path.~~ **The hot-path half of that objection was withdrawn 2026-08-06** when the ingress list moved to `positionCache`: ports now live only in the object's own graph, so a room read never sees them and the lantern's port costs the hot path nothing. **What survives of the objection** is that a per-object graph for something with no interior is close to pure ceremony --- a storage and write-path cost, not a read one. **A third option not yet priced:** the port is *implied* for undecomposed objects and materialized only on first decomposition --- cheap, but it makes presence mean two different things depending on a state nobody declared, which is the kind of split this plan has repeatedly regretted | **Take the uniformity** --- but deliberately, on the fractal argument, not by aesthetics, and with the hot-path cost measured rather than assumed |
| **PQ-10** | **Port-qualified endpoints pass the id predicates and fail the equality checks --- a silent-wrong-answer hazard, verified 2026-08-06.** [`isEphemeraTaggedId`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/baseClasses.ts) splits on `#` and requires exactly two sections, so **`OBJECT#ROPE:1` passes `isEphemeraObjectId`**: one `#`, correct tag. `isEphemeraPositionRelationalEdgeData` therefore accepts the edge as well-formed and it parses into `relationalEdges` --- and then `assertNoRelationalEdgesReferencing`'s `edge.from === id` compares `'OBJECT#ROPE:1'` against `'OBJECT#ROPE'` and **the assert passes vacuously.** `edgeReferencesObjectId` has the same equality and the same hole. **The edge is simultaneously valid to the parser and invisible to the check**, which is worse than a loud rejection. Not architectural --- the matchers must learn to resolve a port-qualified endpoint to its base object, in the same change that introduces ports, never after | Fix the matchers; the open part is **which** sites (at minimum `assertNoRelationalEdgesReferencing`, `edgeReferencesObjectId`, `bothObjectsOnGraph`), and whether resolution belongs in the parse or at each comparison |
| **PQ-8** | **Where do sibling links' licensing labels live?** AB-26's part-connections are labeled by the *licensing spatial relation* --- "connects to the shaft section by way of the 'into the abyss' exit" --- which is **exterior world truth**. So a sibling link sits inside the rope while its label points outward. Referencing an Exit by id is arguably fine (an Exit is an edge, not a graph node, so it is not a port case), but it reopens **AB-28 / AB-29**: repair the floor-hole and the dependent link is now buried a level deeper inside an object | Ports do not help here and may make discovery worse. **This is the loose end in the claim that AB-26 gets a home below the rooms** |
| **PQ-9** | **Numbered or named, declared or derived --- and what separator?** A named port (`ROPE:end-a`) is authored surface area; a numbered one is minted at attachment. Separately: a *declared* port is an explicit interface, a *derived* one re-poses AB-6's stored-versus-derived question one layer down. **Related to PQ-4 but distinct** --- PQ-4 asks about reuse, this asks whether the ordinal means anything. **The separator was filed as cosmetic and is not, per PQ-10.** A `:` is **silently swallowed** by every id predicate in the system; a second `#` makes `isEphemeraTaggedId` **throw** `Illegal nested EphemeraId`, loudly, at every site not yet converted | On the separator: **prefer the one that fails loudly.** Choosing `#` converts a silent-wrong-answer class into a crash class, which is the same instinct as BD-33/35's no-silent-strip rule. On declared-vs-derived: **[single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) leans *derived*** --- a port minted per connection falls out of the connection rather than being authored. It does not foreclose authored ports (a lantern with a hook), but the default now follows from the design. Nothing recorded on numbered-vs-named |

### The motivation, stated plainly (2026-08-06)

**Ports are an attempt to find out whether the design can do without [clause 1](#h1-is-a-conjunction-and-its-clauses-are-tested-separately)** --- because multi-hosting of wholes looks, on reflection, **actually important**. Recorded because this plan's working-hypothesis mechanism depends on knowing *why* a candidate was built, and a scheme designed to make a rival framing viable should not be assessed as though it were neutral.

**This does not withdraw H1**, and nothing below is a decision. What it does is put a fourth, different kind of pressure on clause 1b, which is worth tallying in one place:

1. **The same-mechanism requirement** (AB-32) --- an argument from requirements, producing [the three positions](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-clause-1b-actually-costs) and the narrowed dichotomy.
2. **Ports re-pricing position 1** --- the C7 where-along objection, which was the main charge against multi-hosting, is answered by ports without single-hosting.
3. **PQ-7's restatement** --- the cross-host enforceability cost turns out to belong to **clause 4** (entailed by 1b), and it vanishes under multi-host + ports.
4. **A stated judgment that multi-hosting matters** --- weaker than the others as evidence, and load-bearing as a statement of intent.

**What withdrawal would cost, so the decision is made with the number in view.** The `H1-dependent` set currently stands at **six rows: AB-6, AB-8, AB-9, AB-11, AB-15, AB-17** --- and AB-9's tag covers only the C6 escalation, not its question. That is a small set for a hypothesis this prominent, which is exactly the measurement [the tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) was built to produce: **H1 has been deciding less than its position in this document suggests.** Note also that clause 3 (spanning things are parts) is *not* uniquely H1's and survives a withdrawal intact, as does 1a --- so what is actually at stake is 1b and its two entailments, not the hypothesis wholesale.

**Do not let ports decide this by momentum.** The scheme's independence from any hosting model is its stated virtue; if it also happens to make one model cheaper, that is evidence to weigh, not a conclusion to arrive at by having built it.

### The failure mode all nine are measured against

**An address with no declaration is [the phantom edge](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) one scale down.** Ports exist precisely to give the address a declared home on both sides. Any answer above that leaves a reference implicit, unindexed, or unenforced re-introduces the defect the scheme was built to remove --- which is what makes PQ-6 and PQ-7 the blocking pair rather than two of nine.

---

## Locked frame: parts and ports (2026-08-06)

**A third status, distinct from both "open row" and "working hypothesis."** [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) and [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) were written up as proposals explicitly *not* promoted, because [Working hypotheses](#working-hypotheses) permits only one hypothesis in flight --- two would make the dependent sets overlap and a row tagged twice cannot be rolled back as a unit. **Locking is not promotion.** It does not consume the hypothesis slot; it takes these out of the queue entirely, so the remaining questions --- clause 1b above all --- are decided *inside* this frame rather than alongside it.

**Explicitly not `H3`.** Giving parts and ports hypothesis status would file them back into the rollback machinery, which is the opposite of the move. Nothing below is `H3-dependent`, no tag is minted, and [the tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) is untouched.

### What is locked, at the grain that survives

**Graduated to [`positions/AGENT.concepts.md` --- Wholes, parts, and ports](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) on 2026-08-07, and that is now the durable statement.** The two claims stay below because the rest of this plan argues from them, but the concepts entry is where a reader outside this initiative meets them, and where they survive this file's deletion. **Nothing went to `AGENT.contract.md`** --- none of this is shipped, and contract clauses for unbuilt behavior are the wishlist-normative-text anti-pattern [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md) names. The concepts tier accepts it because its gate is **stability, not shippedness**: it already carries a **Target mental model** section with a graduation rule, and its stated anti-pattern is *unresolved engineering*, which the exclusion list below deliberately keeps out.

Two **shape** claims. Neither names a record format, an identifier scheme, or a hosting model.

1. **A whole has its own graph, with a root node.** Parts are nodes in it; part relations are edges in it.
2. **Boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns** --- not by direct addressing of interior nodes from outside.

**Why these two are safe to fix while everything around them moves:**

| Ground | Where it is argued |
| --- | --- |
| The requirement is **hosting-independent** --- reaching *out* to a whole at a non-local level and reaching *in* to its parts are the same problem, and no hosting decision removes either | [The requirement, stated independently of any hosting model](#the-requirement-stated-independently-of-any-hosting-model) |
| The root node has **three independent derivations** --- AB-3's pre-registered trigger fired from composition, from hosting, and from ports, on three separate lines of reasoning | [The root node is required, not incidental](#the-root-node-is-required-not-incidental) |
| Clause 2's payoff is **encapsulation, not traversal**, and it lands on the two hardest lifecycle rows: interior repartitioning stops being externally breaking | [The strongest argument for ports is not the traversal one](#the-strongest-argument-for-ports-is-not-the-traversal-one) |

**Whole and part are roles, not kinds (added 2026-08-07 from [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)).** Clause 1's wording invites a reading in which an object *is* a whole or *is* a part. It is both, relative to different levels, simultaneously --- a string is a part of a machine and a whole of its spans. **This is a foreclosed misreading, not a lifted clause:** C11 is representable by both clauses and the [re-open criterion](#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause) is not met. But the misreading is not hypothetical --- it produced [clause 1a's falsification](#h1-is-a-conjunction-and-its-clauses-are-tested-separately) --- so **any rule that gives parts and wholes different behaviour is not a rule**, since it assigns two behaviours to one object. Carried into the [concepts entry](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports).

**Lock the scheme with its negative statement attached**, not the sketch alone. [What a port number is not](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) exists because the construct produced two misreadings of the same family within a day --- a reusable-interface-with-fan-out reading, then a port-number-as-part-identity reading, both of which propagated into assessments before being caught. **The design was stable before its durable statement was.** A future reader who takes clause 2 without that section is likely to make the third.

### What is not locked, and must not ride in on this

**The lock is on shape. Everything below is calibration inside the shape, and every one of them is genuinely open.**

| Not locked | Why it stays out |
| --- | --- |
| **[PQ-4](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)** --- port identity and reuse | AB-21's identity obligation one layer down; unchosen between monotonic allocation and keying by what is bound |
| **PQ-9** --- numbered vs named, declared vs derived, **and the separator** | The separator is the one with teeth: per PQ-10, `:` is silently swallowed by every id predicate in the system while a second `#` throws loudly. A shape lock says nothing about which |
| **PQ-13** --- does a plain object carry a presence port | The uniformity argument is good and the ceremony objection survives; deciding it by "well, ports are locked now" would be exactly the momentum this plan warned against. **Re-weighted 2026-08-07 by [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine): still open, but no longer calibration.** If a lantern is present by membership node and a rope by port, the level at which an object switches mechanisms *is* the whole/part line C11 dissolved --- so this is the **base case locked clause 1 rests on**, and it should be decided before anything downstream. **Axis reading, 2026-08-07:** positioning and apprehension are indifferent, mutation charges a small cost, and **reasoning wants one mechanism rather than two** --- which leans yes on a stronger basis than the ceremony objection opposes it. A lean, not a decision |
| **PQ-8** --- where sibling links' licensing labels live | Ports do not help here and may make discovery worse |
| **PQ-12** --- must relocation follow adjacency | Widens AB-26 onto the write side; belongs to that row |
| **PQ-1's record split** --- ***the least locked part of ports*** | Marked settled **provisionally, after one reversal on the day it was made**, and its answer rests on the ingress list living in `positionCache` --- which is **AB-35**, unresolved. If the cache's derivation story comes out differently this reverses again. See [The port record](#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06) |
| **PQ-10** --- the matcher fix | An implementation obligation the lock creates, not a design question it answers. Must land *in* the change that introduces ports, never after |

**The single-use property ([PQ-1's basis](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06)) and the scale-boundary reading ([each side stores its own kind](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06)) sit between the two lists.** They are not shape claims, but nothing currently open depends on their being wrong, and both deflated questions rather than adding them. **Treat them as locked-with-a-lower-bar:** re-openable on a case, not merely on a preference.

### Consequences accepted by locking, recorded so they are owned rather than rediscovered

**AB-6's option space changes rather than narrowing.** [PQ-2](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) found that if a whole's ports enumerate its presence, presence is stored **in the whole** --- a carrier neither of AB-6's two framings (a stored member set, or a predicate derived over graph edges) contemplated. Locking puts a *third* option on that row's table, and it is the one that puts presence and member sets in the same carrier. The row still needs splitting; the split now has three candidates on the presence half, not two.

**AB-25 stays deflated, permanently rather than provisionally.** Sibling links living inside the whole's graph gave AB-26 a home *below* the rooms, which removed most of what Area was being summoned to do above them. That deflation is now a property of the frame. **PQ-8 is the acknowledged loose end in it** --- and it becomes a loose end in something fixed rather than in something still moving, which raises what it costs to be wrong about. Note AB-25 does **not** close: it remains H1's only live falsification trigger, and its character-as-interior-node widening is untouched by anything here.

**The thumb on the clause-1b scale must stay legible.** Ports were designed and priced under "assume multi-host for the moment," and [the motivation section](#the-motivation-stated-plainly-2026-08-06) states plainly that the assumption is not neutral --- ports answered C7's where-along objection without single-hosting, which is one of the four pressures on 1b. **Locking does not add a fifth pressure and must not be read as adding one.** The risk is not that the argument is wrong; it is that a locked-in frame stops looking like an argument at all. Whoever decides 1b should re-read that section knowing ports are fixed, and weigh the pressure at exactly the weight it had before the lock --- no more.

### What the lock delivered for scale-invariance, and where it stops (assessed 2026-08-06)

**The question asked:** [Clean fractal, dirty implementation](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) recorded that the corpus does not fully deliver scale-invariance, and the first pass read it as **more conceptual than practical**. Re-assessed after the lock. **It is now practical below the room boundary and still conceptual above it**, and the two halves should not be reported as one verdict.

**What became practical:**

| Gain | Why it is practical rather than conceptual |
| --- | --- |
| **The recursion is in the type** | Before, a room's graph was a *shard* and an object was a *node* --- different kinds of thing, so a level-crossing walk changed representation as it went. Locked clause 1 makes a whole a graph **and** a node in another graph. `EphemeraPositionGraph` is the single recursive type; "the same relation at every level" stops being a claim about the model and becomes a property of the data |
| **The recursion is composable, not merely repeated** | A fractal with no re-entry construct cannot be traversed. [PQ-5](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) answers yes to chaining and yes to a port being addressable as a part, so a rope in a box in a room resolves by the same hop taken twice --- with `computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has` as the cycle-guard precedent |
| **Invariance across *time*, not only across levels** | ***The largest gain, and it was not what the lock was bought for.*** A thing can change scale --- decompose, reabsorb --- without any external reference knowing what scale it was. AB-17 and AB-31 were failing precisely because external code was bound to a decomposition state. Level-uniform *reading* is the weaker property; this is the stronger one |
| **The clean/dirty discipline gained teeth** | It was a rule with no mechanism behind it, so nothing stopped a shortcut becoming a model change. Coarsening supplies the mechanism --- and in doing so exposed that the discipline was missing a category, now added as **scale-relative truth** |

**Where it stops. Three residues, none of them softened by the lock:**

1. **The fractal is invariant below the room and something else above it.** `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is `Room | Character`. The lock says *objects* are ported graphs; it says nothing about rooms, and **AB-25**'s Room/Area containment is a structure of different provenance --- authored asset-stack merge rather than play-time graph mutation. A walk upward from a battery still changes representational regime at the room boundary. **AB-25's character-as-interior-node widening sharpens this rather than softening it:** characters are hosts, an LCA of a room and a character is needed, and nothing says a character is a ported graph. **This is the next boundary that would have to be locked for the fractal claim to be general.** **Partially softened the same day by [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system):** a moonbase computer system ladders *above* human scale while remaining an `OBJECT#`, which suggests the fractal extends upward without Room/Area needing to be ported at all. **Read this residue with that case beside it** --- the room/area *provenance* problem is untouched, but "the fractal stops at the room" is stated more absolutely here than the corpus now supports.
2. **AB-13's DAG is untouched**, exactly as the clean/dirty table already classifies it. Chaining plus multiple parents makes traversal an unambiguous DAG walk rather than a tree recursion --- workable, and a permanent qualifier on the word *fractal*.
3. **Write-side scale change has no vocabulary at all.** One identity changing scale is now clean; one identity **becoming two** is undefined, and `divide`/`merge` are [handed to the ladder](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) and blocked on **AB-31**.

**And the base case is not decided.** [PQ-13](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) is deliberately outside the lock, and it *is* the practical invariance test: does a lantern run the same code with zero hops? The uniformity argument there is the one that rescued [position 2](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-clause-1b-actually-costs) --- *a loop that sometimes iterates zero times is one mechanism* --- so it is likely to land, but until it does the bottom of the ladder is as open as the top.

**The risk the lock introduces, recorded so it is watched rather than discovered.** Encapsulation means external code cannot see what scale a thing is at --- which is exactly what invariance requires, and also exactly what would let a real level asymmetry go unremarked. **The room/area seam is now less visible, not less real.** The failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained. Residue 1 is that reader's warning, and it should survive into `AGENT.concepts.md` whenever this graduates. **Discharged 2026-08-07** --- it travels with the [concepts entry](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) as a required warning rather than waiting for Phase 6, since the entry is exactly what would otherwise be read as a uniformity claim.

### What would re-open a locked clause

Not a preference, not a cheaper-looking alternative, and not a rival proposal that happens to also work. **A case in the corpus that either clause cannot represent** --- the standard [C7 met](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) --- or a demonstration that clause 2's encapsulation claim fails where it was bought, which means an interior repartitioning that still breaks external references. Either goes in [Discussion record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#discussion-record) with the case attached, and the lock is lifted by name rather than eroded by exception.

---

## Proposal P4: settling, dataflow, and narrative heat

**Recorded 2026-08-07 from conversation, and it is the largest piece of design in this initiative that until now existed only there.** ~~**Not promoted to a working hypothesis**, for the reason [candidate H2](#candidate-h2-not-adopted-ab-12s-privileged-level-reframe) gives and [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) and [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) already follow: H1 is still in flight, and two hypotheses make the dependent sets overlap.~~ **That reason no longer holds --- H1 died the same day this was written, and P3 was promoted into H3 for exactly the same reason.**

**Status: accepted 2026-08-07, as a Prototype, not a Proposal or a Working Hypothesis --- deliberately a third category.** See [What P4 being load-bearing here means](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) for why the name matters and not only the label. The heading above is left as **Proposal P4** rather than renamed, per this plan's supersession rule and to keep every existing anchor into this section valid --- read "Proposal" in the heading as historical, not current.

**Provenance, and it predates this initiative by a long way.** The scheme comes from the **Ludic Network** conception worked out when `Variable` and `Compute` elements were abandoned --- *a connected graph-of-graphs of story elements*. The conflict it was built to resolve is the one [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable) has now reached from the practical side. **Recorded as prior conception rather than as a new idea**, because a design arriving twice by different routes is evidence, and because this plan has twice mistaken a remembered claim for a verified one. **Checked 2026-08-07 for scalability precedent and found silent, not favorable:** `Variable`/`Compute` were abandoned for a *flexibility* reason --- a deterministic JS cascade couldn't address unexpected player input, and the JS itself was a black box no LLM step could sensibly read --- not a scaling one; by the author's own account it scaled fine. **This history neither supports nor undermines P4's scalability**, and should not be read as either.

### What P4 being load-bearing here means (2026-08-07)

**The pattern to watch for is named in H1's own write-up, and it is happening again.** [Trigger 2's conclusion](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) warned that "conditional adoption goes de facto permanent once the dependent set is large enough that rollback means redoing fifteen answers --- at which point [a hypothesis] survives on accumulated cost rather than merit." Three rows lean on P4: **AB-39** (opened first, "if it falls this question returns intact"), and, added the same day as this section, **AB-40(b)** and **AB-37**, both narrowed by [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork) toward P4's topology-change/settling answer for whole-summary upkeep.

**Accepted, and accepted as staged --- iteration 1 only, not the full scheme.** [Discussed and agreed 2026-08-07:] P4 is a natural fit for iterative implementation, because the concrete grounding later iterations need (real fan-out, real diamond frequency, real settle time) is discoverable only by running an early one, not by further design-time reasoning. Four iterations sketched, in order: **(1)** a naive everything-at-once cascade, single invocation, committed in **sequential batches rather than one atomic transaction** --- this is what keeps it distinct from the path [the problem table](#the-problem-both-obvious-answers-are-unbounded-and-one-of-them-is-impossible) already rules out as structurally impossible, since each batch still declares its own footprint even though the scheduling across batches is naive. Untenable at scale; workable for a prototype the size of CoyoteGame; buys the partial-completion resilience that makes committing in batches safe at all, which nothing later can skip. **(2)** split into separately scheduled invocations, naive ordering --- this, not iteration 1, is what actually unparks the unshipped `thinking` `claimed`-status worker noted in [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark). **(3)** heat-maps, used to influence scheduling. **(4)** decay and re-energize --- the largest *conceptual* jump (lossless eventual consistency becomes a lossy, attention-driven cascade) but, by that point, not a large *implementation* one.

**Only iteration 1 is designed now; only iteration 1 is what the three dependent rows may cite.** AB-40(b) and AB-37 need nothing beyond "a self-maintaining, eventually-consistent cascade exists, with a stated partial-completion guarantee" --- iteration 1's whole content, no heat or decay required. AB-39 is only partly cleared: iteration 1 gives it a concrete "against which snapshot" answer (the last completed batch), but its PS-1/PS-3 sub-questions are about the reachability mechanism, not the cascade's staging, and stay open regardless of how many iterations P4 eventually has. **Tag dependent rows `P4-i1-dependent`, not `P4-dependent`** --- a row citing the full scheme when it only needs iteration 1's guarantee is the same over-tagging error [the tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) already warns against, one proposal over.

**Why "Prototype" and not "Proposal," and it is not a labeling nicety.** H1's rollback, had it come, cost **design time only** --- reasoning re-done, rows reverted to Open, no code touched, because H1 was never built. **P4-iteration-1 is going to have code behind it before iterations 2--4 are designed.** If it fails, the rollback is not "revert some Status cells" --- it is deciding what happens to shipped code that was known, going in, to be non-scalable. That is a heavier and more dangerous failure mode than H1's, not a different-flavored version of the same one: sunk *engineering* cost pulls harder toward keeping something that isn't working than sunk *reasoning* cost ever did, which is exactly why this needed its own category rather than riding on the Working-hypothesis vocabulary built for a design-only bet.

**The gamble is accepted on the following basis, stated so it can be checked against later rather than re-litigated:** further design-time analysis has run out of leverage --- the one available piece, "this is the same family as spreadsheets and circuit simulation," is real supporting evidence that a scalable version is *possible in principle* (those systems scale via exactly iteration 2/3's bounded-batch-then-prioritized-recompute shape) but cannot say whether *our* graph, with C6's diamonds and C12's directed causal edges layered over an LLM-authored consequence shape, behaves like theirs. Only running iteration 1 answers that. **Iteration 1 must therefore be built to measure, not only to work:** it should report cascade fan-out, diamond frequency, and settle time under naive scheduling as a first-class output, not an afterthought, since that data is what iterations 2 and 3 are designed from and the only way to obtain it short of building them blind. **A fourth measurement added 2026-08-09: fault propagation** --- see [the feedback regime](#the-feedback-regime-is-the-thing-to-measure-and-it-decides-which-solutions-to-look-for-2026-08-09). Proxies rather than direct measurement: how often a later reasoning step contradicts an earlier established fact, how deep summarization chains actually run, and whether contradiction frequency **rises with world age**. **That last one is the damping-versus-amplifying question stated as something countable**, and iteration 1 is the first place it can be asked of real data rather than argued.

**Named rollback trigger, set now while it costs nothing to be honest about:** if iteration 1's measured fan-out does not visibly compress under a sketched heat-driven redesign --- i.e., prioritized recompute would still have to touch most of what naive scheduling touches --- P4 as a whole is the thing that failed, not iteration 3's design. **AB-39, AB-40(b), and AB-37 all revert to Open if that happens**, exactly as `P4-i1-dependent` tagging exists to make cheap to discover. **P4 also has known, named costs independent of this trigger** (diamond glitches on DAG membership, cross-noted at H3 clause 6, and oscillation on causal cycles) that iteration 1's instrumentation should surface evidence on, not merely inherit as accepted risk by default.

### The problem: both obvious answers are unbounded, and one of them is impossible

| Path | Cost | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Store all cascading updates immediately on change** | Unbounded write fan-out | **Not merely expensive --- structurally impossible on the shipped kernel.** `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs **once**, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered, so a transaction must declare every host it touches *before* walking. **A cascade whose extent is discovered by walking cannot declare its footprint** |
| **Re-derive from first sources on each read** | Unbounded fetch --- and after [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine), unbounded in *depth* as well, since no level is privileged | Workable, and pays on the player-facing path |

**That asymmetry is what makes the third path structural rather than preferential.** It is not the compromise between two workable options; it is the only shape that fits a kernel with a once-only footprint.

### The scheme

**Impulses of causal change are persisted into the graph as data.** Repeated waves of processing continue a chain of consequence that earlier passes carried only partway. **The consistency guarantee is given neither at write time nor lazily at read time, but *eventually*, when the graph's energy settles.**

**What it actually buys, stated so nobody defends it as an efficiency:** total work does not go down --- waves re-process, so it likely goes up. What changes is **which cost is bounded**. One unbounded transaction becomes many bounded ones, each able to declare a footprint it can know. **Unbounded *synchronous* becomes bounded synchronous plus unbounded *asynchronous*** --- the right trade precisely because the synchronous path is the player-facing one.

### The substrate is half-built, and the missing half is exactly this half

Verified against [`thinking/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/thinking/AGENT.md) when this plan mined it on 2026-08-05, before any of this was in view:

- **Shipped:** durable `Meta::Schedule` rows on `TASK#${workItemId}`, the status vocabulary `scheduled | claimed | cancelled | completed`, and a retention policy that explicitly does **not** TTL in-flight rows, so *"opportunistically queued work is not TTL'd while waiting."*
- **Not shipped:** **nothing writes `claimed`.** No worker, no deferred dispatch, no queue anything pulls from.

**A persisted impulse is a durable worklist entry, and a durable worklist is what those rows are.** `Meta::Result`'s `nonCollidingPutItem` --- first write wins, duplicate finalize returns `alreadyFinalized` --- supplies the idempotence a wave system needs when a wave is processed twice.

**Note the pattern rather than the instance.** This is the sixth time this initiative has found infrastructure pre-registered for a need it had not yet articulated. **The rule that follows: look for the pre-registration before designing anything new here.**

### Retraction dissolves under a dataflow reading, and that reading is the ludic-native one

**The problem as first posed.** Turning power *on* propagates naturally; turning it *off* after a cut is a **retraction**, and a naive relaxation never un-powers the door because nothing gives the door a reason to re-examine itself. That is the classic truth-maintenance problem, and it is genuinely harder than the direction the scheme handles well.

**The reframe, and it is a different computational model rather than a restatement:** *turning off the power is not the retraction of a positive state --- it is its own change, propagating as any other change does.*

| | Truth-maintenance | **Dataflow** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What a node holds | Facts, with justifications | **A current value** |
| Removing support | Find dependents, unwind --- absence has no representation to propagate | **Does not arise.** A new value arrives and flows |
| What justifies a value | A tracked justification structure | **The graph itself** --- every dependent is visited by construction |

**Under dataflow there is no retraction, only a new value.** Spreadsheets, reactive systems and circuit simulation are the right family. **The graph holds *states*, not *beliefs*** --- and "retraction" was an artifact of modelling the world as a knowledge base rather than as a world. **Same instinct that produced *ludic* as a name, arriving at the algorithm instead of the vocabulary.**

**Where the cost went, because it did not vanish.** Dataflow is **eager** where truth-maintenance can be lazy: cutting the trunk visits every downstream device, including the ones nobody will look at. Total work becomes proportional to the **graph**, not to what anyone asked. **Here that is the right trade rather than merely a different one** --- eager full fan-out is exactly what waves are good at, since each frontier is bounded and declarable. It is also what [narrative heat](#narrative-heat-the-optimization-that-is-probably-not-only-an-optimization) exists to prune.

#### The one wrinkle that survives: topology change

**Value changes propagate. Topology changes do not, because the thing that would carry the impulse is the thing being removed.**

The generator going dark is a value change on a node --- push it downstream. **Cutting the cable is an edge deletion**, and in a push model there is no longer an edge to push along. Nothing tells the door it lost an input.

**The rule that follows: edge deletion emits its own impulse before it ceases to exist.** The cut knows both endpoints; it delivers *you lost an input* to the downstream one as part of the cut, then removes the edge.

**And the codebase already forbids silent edge disappearance.** `removeObject` **asserts and throws** when a relational edge still references a node, deliberately with no silent-strip variant (BD-33/35), and [`boundaryEdgeOutcomes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) classifies every severed edge **carry / dissolve / defer / error**. **Fifth arrival of the boundary-edge discipline in a case outside its brief** --- and the least expected, since it was written for carrying a chest through a doorway.

#### Two defects inherited with the model

1. **Glitches on diamonds.** A node with two inputs tracing to a common ancestor can be reached by the short path before the long one and briefly compute a value that was never true. **[AB-13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s DAG makes this real rather than theoretical** --- one part, two wholes, is the diamond. Fixes: topological ordering of the worklist, or accepting transient glitches.
2. **Oscillation on cycles.** Generator powers pump, pump cools generator, and that is ordinary fiction. **Composition is a DAG; the causal kind is not**, so termination needs a guard rather than a structural guarantee --- `computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has` is the shipped precedent. The dataflow framing does not fix cycles but it makes the failure **legible**: oscillation you can detect rather than regress you cannot.

### Narrative heat: the optimization that is probably not only an optimization

**The waste, made concrete.** [Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) strung through thirty labyrinth rooms. A naive cascade --- impulses along every link, no decay, no differentiation --- eventually proposes *"the string goes briefly taut"* to all thirty. **In actual play most of those rooms are empty**, and that calculation is the noise of a tree falling in an unoccupied forest.

**The scheme, in two halves.**

1. **The network cascades a second quantity besides causality: *narrative heat*.** Places people are generate more heat than empty rooms; things people are interacting with are hotter than things they ignore. **Heat cascades as a heat map in the *reverse* direction of links.**
2. **Change-impulses carry an energy payload** that **decays over traverse**, **splits on splits**, and **reinforces as it encounters heat** --- so impulses stochastically **travel toward heat**.

**Prior art, named because it tells us where the failure modes are.** Closest is **importance sampling with Russian-roulette termination** from path tracing, which faces this exact shape --- unbounded transport, most contributions negligible --- and has a rigorous answer about when stochastic termination is *safe*: kill paths probabilistically, but scale survivors so the expected result stays exact. **Unbiased, not merely cheap.** Also adjacent: ant-colony pheromone gradients (reverse-propagated attractant plus stochastic traversal, nearly one-to-one) and attention weighting.

#### The distinction the scheme requires: narration versus state

**Two different things travel the same links, and decay is safe for only one of them.**

| Impulse kind | Example | Decay in a cold room |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Transient / narration** | *"the string goes briefly taut"* | **Free and correct.** Nobody hears it, nothing persists. This is the tree in the empty forest, and the argument for heat is exactly right |
| **Persistent / state** | *"the string is now cut"* | **Lossy.** Segment 27 stays wrong --- and stays wrong **forever**, surfacing an hour later when someone walks in and finds a taut string that should be slack |

**This is a property of the impulse, not of the edge.** The same `part-of` link carries both, so it belongs in its own column rather than folding into [the relation-kind register](#the-relation-kind-register).

#### Heat rising must pull unsettled state to settle

**The repair falls out of the same mechanism, which is the main reason to think the scheme is sound rather than merely clever.** A character entering a cold room **is** a heat spike --- and it is also **the read**, the exact moment the state must be correct.

**So heat works in both directions in time:** it guides impulses forward in space, and a heat *increase* reaches back and finishes the ones that stalled. That is lazy evaluation reintroduced precisely at the boundary where laziness is correct, and it is what keeps cold regions from diverging permanently.

**It locks together with the tri-state.** A room whose state is `unsettled` is a room whose pending impulses must resolve before it answers. **Fourth independent arrival at that requirement** --- after coarsening ([C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable)), reads-during-settling, and Coyote observability --- and the first where it is load-bearing rather than tidy.

#### Decay to *parked*, never to *dead* (PROVISIONAL: challenged on two counts)

**Stochastic termination has a failure mode that reads terribly in a game:** *the trap door did not fire because we rolled badly.* Non-reproducible, undebuggable, and --- in the prototype setting --- indistinguishable from a dropped wave, which is itself indistinguishable from comic timing.

**So split the stochasticity:** **stochastic in scheduling priority** (which impulses process first --- where the optimization actually lives), **deterministic in termination** (an impulse below threshold is **parked**, never discarded). A parked impulse is a durable low-priority worklist entry that a heat rise promotes.

**And the shipped retention policy already describes exactly this:** in-flight `Meta::Schedule` rows *"do not set `deleteAt`, so opportunistically queued work is not TTL'd while waiting."* **A parked impulse waiting for heat is opportunistically queued work.**

**Recorded as provisional 2026-08-07.** Two objections, and they are not objections to parking --- they are objections to *never dead*, which is a stronger claim than the argument above earns.

**Objection 1: the parked set is an unbounded history.** Nothing above bounds it. It grows with every below-threshold impulse the world has ever produced, and the cost is not primarily storage --- it is that **a heat rise promotes the whole accumulation at once.** A room that goes cold for a simulated year and then has a character walk into it faces a settle proportional to a year of events, at exactly the moment a read is waiting. The mechanism advertised as the cheap path has its worst case precisely where its correctness guarantee is invoked.

**Objection 2: it aspires to the wrong discipline.** *Never dead* is a promise that **every causal lead is eventually followed up, regardless of whether anyone ever cared.** That is not how fiction works and not how a world should be run. There has to be a way to say **"this happened, it might have mattered if anyone had cared, nobody did, and we are not going to keep pushing it"** --- and the scheme as written has no such statement, only indefinite deferral.

**What the original argument actually supported.** The trap-door complaint is about **silent, non-reproducible** loss. It is satisfied by a *recorded* termination --- *this impulse decayed below threshold, here, then* --- not by keeping the impulse **live**. Those are different properties, and the section conflated them:

| Property | What it buys | Was it argued for? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Auditable** --- termination is recorded | Kills the undebuggable-roll failure mode | **Yes.** This is the whole of the trap-door argument |
| **Revivable** --- a heat rise can still promote it | Cold regions converge on read | **No.** Asserted alongside, never separately justified --- and it is the one the objections hit |

**Three repair directions, none adopted.** Recorded so the next pass argues rather than re-derives:

1. **Collapse rather than queue.** [The dataflow reframe](#retraction-dissolves-under-a-dataflow-reading-and-that-reading-is-the-ludic-native-one) already says *the graph holds states, not beliefs* --- so parked work should be a **pending delta per node**, not a log of impulses. Fifty queued changes to segment 27 collapse to *what segment 27 should now be*. That bounds the parked set by **graph size × relation kind**, not by event count, and it kills objection 1 outright. **This is a consequence of the reframe that was already recorded and not drawn** --- which is reason to suspect it is right, and reason to check it is not merely tidy.
2. **Park only what is not rederivable.** Under dataflow most state is recomputable from current sources, and rederivation from a cold region is affordable *because it is rare and local* --- the trilemma's second path, readmitted at the one scale where it was never the expensive option. If a node can be rederived on heat rise, its parked impulses are redundant and may be discarded. **Parking is then mandatory only for facts with no surviving cause record** --- *the cable was cut* --- which is a much smaller set.
3. **Let absence of heat be terminal, not merely deferring.** The mechanism objection 2 asks for **already exists in the scheme** --- it is heat's absence. What is missing is *permission for it to end something*. The candidate rule: an impulse parked below threshold past some horizon **expires**, auditably, and the node it targeted is marked rederive-on-read rather than settle-on-read.

**And the narration/state split above probably already decides most of it.** Dropping a **transient/narration** impulse nobody was present for is not a defect --- it is correct, and permanently correct; the loss the table describes is entirely in the **persistent/state** column. If so, *never dead* was over-generalised from the one row that needed it. **Not asserted --- it is [PS-11](#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused).**

#### Split versus replicate is a per-relation-kind fact

**Energy dividing at a branch is conservation --- right for a *pull*, where force genuinely divides between paths. Wrong for a power cut**, which is a broadcast: all thirty downstream devices go dark and none goes one-thirtieth dark. Attenuating across a fan-out would leave a large power system unable to notify its own edges.

**So split-versus-replicate tracks whether the underlying fiction conserves something**, and it is a register entry. **It is also the register's first *prediction* rather than a recorded observation**, which is what that section was opened hoping it would do.

#### Heat's own cost, and a tension to resolve before building it

Heat is itself a reverse cascade from every occupied room, and its source term is **character navigation --- the highest-frequency mutation in the system** (BD-36; it routes through the same `commitStepSequence` as take/drop). On the face of it, one unbounded cascade has become two.

**What saves it: heat has no correctness obligation.** A heat map stale by seconds is fine; a causal state stale by seconds is a bug. So heat may be batched, timer-decayed, approximated, and shed under load --- none of which causality may do. **The asymmetry is what makes the second cascade affordable, and it should be stated as a rule before someone tries to make heat exact.**

**But heat is probably not only an optimization**, and that is where the tension is. *What does anyone care about right now* is the signal at least four things have been reaching for: [C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name)'s Coyote context, which stages a whole room relevant or not and has no relevance filter; **AB-22**'s affinity filter; **AB-24**'s ambient context tier; and the [reasoning axis](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to) generally, since what belongs in an LLM packet is the hot material. Apprehension wants it too --- what gets described in detail is the hot material.

**Those two claims are in direct tension.** A mechanism several correctness-adjacent consumers depend on cannot be as sloppy as the paragraph above says it is allowed to be. **Resolve that before heat is built, not after** --- most likely by separating a cheap approximate heat used for scheduling from whatever the context consumers actually need, and confirming they are not the same artifact.

### Why CoyoteGame is the prototype, and the seam in that choice

**The artifact this architecture produces is a delay:** a trap door opens, nothing happens for several seconds, and *then* the person standing on it falls. **In most settings that is a defect. In cartoon slapstick it is the genre's defining comic beat**, so the prototype domain was chosen partly because the architecture's characteristic failure is a genre-confirming win there.

**A second, independent reason points at the same prototype**, and it is the stronger one for the record: **[C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name) establishes Coyote is also the domain that most needs the structure** --- `CoyoteStagedObject` carries no edges at all, so a rope tied to a branch and the same objects in a heap produce **byte-identical** prompt context.

**Three seams in the choice, recorded because a comfortable prototype is where measurements do not get taken.**

1. **The genre that forgives the delay never measures it.** Nothing in Coyote will complain that settling took eight seconds rather than eight hundred milliseconds. **Instrument settle time from the first wave anyway** --- Coyote is the only place to measure it under real load without the pressure to fix it first.
2. **In Coyote the delay is load-bearing, so Coyote will push to *preserve* it** --- and then to make it *reliable*, because a gag needs the beat to land consistently rather than varying with worker scheduling. **A reliable beat is a *scheduled* delay, a completely different mechanism from an incidental one.** Keep them separate by construction: incidental latency is an artifact to minimise; a deliberate comic beat is a narration- or trope-level device that asks for itself. Otherwise genre content ends up tuned into the causal substrate --- the instance-taken-for-the-category error already twice in this plan's catalogue.
3. **A dropped impulse and a gag that has not landed yet look identical**, and so does non-termination. **This is the third argument for making settle state explicit** rather than inferring it from *nothing has happened yet*.

**And one consequence for sequencing.** Both motivating cases --- the cut cable and the trap door --- are **removals of support**. That is not coincidence: assertion is fictionally boring and technically easy, while removal is where the drama is and where the algorithm is hard. **Slapstick is almost entirely removal of support**, so the hard direction is what the prototype hits on day one rather than in a later phase. Plan the first slice accordingly.

### What P4 would do to rollback trigger 3

**Moot as originally posed, 2026-08-07: H1 is dead, and not by this trigger firing.** Rollback trigger 3 asked what happens if *the migration cascade proves unplannable* under H1's clause 4/5 whole-migration. [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) has no whole-migration to be unplannable --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) already showed coiling a rope rewrites port egress with no whole ever moving. There is no cascade here for P4 to rescue, because the thing that would have needed rescuing no longer exists.

**Left in place rather than deleted, because its second paragraph turned out to be the more important half and was vindicated independently.** *"Eventual consistency on presence is a different and less obviously acceptable proposition than eventual consistency on state, and it should be argued on its own rather than inherited"* --- written 2026-08-06, before C13 existed. **This is exactly the distinction AB-40 split on 2026-08-07** (see [Open decisions](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)), from a completely different direction: taking the fork (presence/position) must stay synchronous, while what `PlaceSetting`'s summary says about it (state/consequence) can settle. Two independent arguments, a day apart, landing on the same line --- worth noting as the kind of convergence this plan has already shown repeatedly (AB-28, AB-29, and C5's guard among them), not as a coincidence to skip past.

### Open questions (P4 sub-questions; ids stable, never reused)

**Parallel to P3's PQ series, and none of these is settled.**

| ID | Question |
