| **AB-38** | **Does "Improvisation" name the mechanism or one of AB-14's two triggers?** **Split out of AB-0 on 2026-08-07** when the naming half locked; the ID is new because IDs are stable and never reused, and the question is unchanged --- it is **not** resolved by *Abstraction Fractal*, which answers the concept's name and says nothing about the mechanism's. **The collision, restated once so this row stands alone.** [Improvisation is the objects lane's mechanism, generalized](#improvisation-is-the-objects-lanes-mechanism-generalized) establishes that minting an unauthored component is a **shipped** mechanism raised to a general one. But AB-14 also uses **Improvisation** for the **downward, operation-driven trigger**, paired with **Recognition** as the upward one. **The word does both jobs** --- the same conflation AB-14 was restructured to remove --- and if Improvisation is the mechanism then Improvisation and Recognition are **not peers**: Recognition is a trigger that invokes it, and operational necessity is the other trigger. **Two ways out, unchanged:** **(a)** Improvisation and Recognition stay the two triggers and the shared mechanism takes its own name; **(b)** Improvisation names the mechanism and the downward trigger is renamed. **Why it did not ride out on AB-0's lock, deliberately:** the corpus evidence that settled the concept name (no privileged level, roles not kinds) bears on *levels*, not on *triggers*, so nothing that resolved AB-0 touches this. Resolving it by momentum is the move the lock's own warning forbids. **One thing has changed since it was raised.** The corpus now tags every case by trigger, and **three of eleven fire *neither*** --- [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system) and [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) because they are **authored**, [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) because it is **lifecycle**. That is evidence the trigger vocabulary is incomplete *independently* of what the mechanism is called, and it leans (a): a mechanism named separately from its triggers can gain a third trigger without renaming anything. **A lean, not a decision.** | AB-14, AB-19, AB-21 | **Open --- inherited live from AB-0; (a) leans on new evidence** |
| **AB-39** | **When is a causal state answer evaluated, and against which snapshot?** Opened 2026-08-07 from [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable), which established that *is the door powered* is **reachability through intact edges**, not a lookup --- so something must run it, at some point, over some version of the graph. **Distinct from the two rows it will be mistaken for.** **AB-6** asks stored-vs-derived about **member sets**; this asks when a *computation over* the members runs. **AB-35** governs `positionCache` derivation and invalidation for **positional** expansion; a causal answer invalidates on a different event (any edge cut anywhere on the path, not a host change) and has no natural cache unit. Conflating either would hide the difference. **The hard constraint is already known:** `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs **once**, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered --- so an evaluation whose result determines *which hosts an operation touches* must complete before the footprint is fixed. A door that opens only if powered is exactly that shape. **One existing pattern is likely the answer and should be tested before anything is invented:** this codebase already re-checks across snapshots, evaluating at dry-run and again at commit, and treats the recheck as a **safety property rather than duplicated work**. If a causal answer is cheap to recompute, that pattern applies directly. **The open sub-questions:** what the evaluation unit is (a system? a path? a query?), whether a fault short-circuits the walk, what an answer means when the graph is mid-transaction, and --- from C12's finding 3 --- **whether an unreachable address returns *unknown* rather than the whole's answer**, since coarsening a reachability fact is unsound rather than merely coarse. | AB-6, AB-35, AB-1, P4 iteration 1 | **Open, `P4-i1-dependent` in part --- largely absorbed by [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat) the same week:** under settling the answer is *against the last settled state, plus pending impulses*, which iteration 1 alone supplies. The row's live residue, **PS-1** and **PS-3**, is about the reachability mechanism, not the cascade's staging, and stays open at any iteration depth. **Do not close it into P4** --- P4-iteration-1 is now an accepted Prototype rather than an unpromoted proposal, but it is still a bet with code behind it and a [named rollback trigger](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07); if it fires, this question returns intact |
| **AB-40** | **The detach/reattach step kind --- and the row split in two, 2026-08-07.** Opened from [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork). **(a) The structural move.** A part's node leaving `PlaceSetting`'s interior graph and landing in the character's --- this must stay **synchronous**: a player needs to reference and use the fork the instant it is taken, so deferring the relocation to eventual consistency would be a real gameplay defect, not a modelling nicety. **Reframed twice now.** First, narrower than initially stated: not a new step *kind*, but whether the existing transfer step can address a node inside another object's interior graph as a source or destination at all. **Second, 2026-08-07: not an open design question at all.** There is no candidate answer being weighed against another --- AB-11's Expansion answer was never a competing proposal for this, it simply doesn't reach here. This is scoped, known-needed, unbuilt functionality, which is what [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) exists to track and [Phase 5](#recommended-order) exists to schedule --- not a fork for this table to carry. **(b) The consequence.** What `PlaceSetting`'s own summary says about itself once the fork is gone, and the reverse when it returns --- **answered, in direction if not in detail, by [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)'s topology-change rule**: the `part` edge's deletion emits its own impulse (*you lost a member*) exactly as a cut cable does in C12, and a settling wave, not a synchronous step, is what eventually regenerates the summary. **This does not need to be fast** --- the character already knows they hold the fork; the room description catching up half a beat later is what settling exists for. Narrows **AB-37** with a second, independent case reaching the same answer as AB-6's contested note. | AB-11, AB-4, AB-6, AB-17, AB-37, H3 clause 7, P4 iteration 1 | **Split, and only (b) still belongs in this table.** (a) is **not a design fork** --- moved to [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) as scoped, unbuilt kernel capability, to be scheduled through Phase 5 rather than decided here. (b) is **`P4-i1-dependent`**: conceptually resolved by iteration 1's accepted guarantee alone, no heat or decay needed --- reverts to Open if P4's [named rollback trigger](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) fires |
| **AB-41** | **Ephemeral / virtual abstractions.** Opened 2026-08-07 from [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up). Construct an H3-shaped whole (root, parts, ports) **in memory, for the duration of one operation, never written to the graph** --- the same computation `computeCarryClosure` already does, retyped as AB-3's already-anticipated collapse rather than a bespoke fragment. **Answers AB-18's "a carry closure?" scope candidate with a concrete trigger**: the operation itself (pick up), not arrangement detection. **Dissolves the vacuous-abstraction threshold question C14 opened with**: constructing the ephemeral form is always cheap and safe, so there is no firing threshold to tune --- the open question moves entirely to *promotion* (ephemeral to durable), which C14 argues is [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)'s narrative heat's job (iteration 3, not built) rather than a new mechanism. **Distinct from AB-30**: AB-30's wall is addressable-never-a-member *permanently*, as an edge; this is addressable-never-a-member *for one operation's duration*, as a whole. **Noted, not developed:** the same construct plausibly serves [C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name)'s reasoning-context packaging problem, a use on the reasoning axis rather than the mutation axis this row was opened from. | AB-3, AB-6, AB-18, AB-30, H3 clause 3, P4 | **Open --- newly named; construction (via H3's whole shape) has a clear answer, promotion criterion is deferred to P4 iteration 3 and not yet actionable** |
| **AB-42** | **Are established negatives ludic-graph nodes, or a collection beside the graph?** Opened 2026-08-09 from conversation, following [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about), which established that the **negative record is the one thing storage lacks** --- established-present mints rows and *not established* is correctly represented by nothing, so this row is the whole of the storage question. **Two candidates. (a) A negative collection** owned by the room or the whole --- the obvious shape, cheap, and no new node kind. **(b) Negatives are nodes in the ludic graph**, carrying ordinary relation-kind edges like any other node. **The argument for (b) is one this plan has already made, one level up, and it is the strongest thing on the table.** [P2](#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge)'s conceptual error is *hosting creates a phantom edge*: membership expressed by **containment in a collection** rather than by an edge, leaving a link that is "not merely unlabeled but **untypeable** --- the edge could not be written into `edges` even without a kind, because one endpoint has no node to be." **A negative list is that shape exactly**, a scalar sibling of `nodes`/`edges` holding facts that cannot be edge endpoints --- so (a) recreates the phantom this plan diagnosed and set out to remove, one scale down and *after* the diagnosis. **The discriminating requirement is causal, and it is what opened the row:** absence has measurable causal effect --- a flashlight gives no light, deducible from *no battery or power source* **without knowing the flashlight's other details**. Under [P4](#retraction-dissolves-under-a-dataflow-reading-and-that-reading-is-the-ludic-native-one)'s dataflow reading, consequence propagates **along edges between nodes holding values**; a negative that is not a node cannot be an endpoint, so the cascade must consult a second structure that is not the graph --- the phantom again, now on the hot path. **[H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) clause 5 says the same from the boundary side:** ports bind any relation kind crossing a boundary, and a negative *inside* a whole with consequence *outside* it (no battery, flashlight dark, read from the room) needs an interior end for a port to bind to. A collection entry has none. **Two real costs on (b), neither fatal and both unpriced.** **(i) Accumulation, and it cuts against the initiative's own purpose.** Negatives are cheaper to create than positives --- *no* costs a sentence, *yes* costs a mint --- so they accrue faster, and every traversal, summary and LLM packet pays for them. A kitchen carrying five knives and five hundred *there is no X* nodes is **worse** reasoning context than one carrying five knives, which inverts the bounding the abstraction layer exists to provide. **(ii) A negative is not an `OBJECT#`** --- no `shortName`, no body, no placement --- and node kinds are tag-discriminated `'Character' \| 'Object'` today, so (b) needs a third tag. **It may be the first thing this initiative has found that genuinely cannot be an Object**, which *pressures* AB-12's everything-is-an-Object reframe rather than being absorbed by it. **Restated 2026-08-09 under [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), and the question is now about *licences* rather than about negatives.** **Both recorded costs move favourably.** *Accumulation* collapses --- one licence per entity whose content narrows, rather than one node per negative, and a licence **coarsens** where independent negative nodes do not. *Node-kind* becomes principled rather than awkward: a **licence** node is an ordinary modelling construct where a **negative** node was a third tag with no natural reading, so the pressure on AB-12 is real but no longer strange. **And P5 supplies a second argument for (b)** independent of P2's phantom: a licence is *manipulable* --- widened, narrowed, delegated --- and a thing that changes in play wants to be an edge endpoint. | AB-12, AB-43, AB-44, AB-21, P2, P4, P5, H3 clause 5 | **Open --- (b) strengthened; both costs cheaper under P5, and the question is now "how is a licence represented," not "how is a negative represented"** |
| **AB-43** | **What is an established negative keyed by, when its referent does not exist?** Opened 2026-08-09. **This is [AB-21](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s identity obligation in its hardest form.** *"Is there a deep fryer"* and *"is there a deep-fat fryer"* must reach the **same** negative, or the world answers inconsistently and the record does no work at all --- but there is no id to key on, because nothing exists. `stableKey` lives on `Meta::Object`, and there is no `Meta::Object`. **Candidates, none assessed:** a normalized phrase; a semantic vector (the objects lane already carries `EMBEDDING#IMPROMPTU`, so this **inherits improvisation gap 3** --- whether a `shortName`-derived vector is the right text --- in a harder form, since there is no `shortName` either); a term from a closed type vocabulary; or keying **the question** rather than the thing. **Note the closed-vocabulary trap explicitly:** an enumerated list is only correct where the vocabulary is genuinely enumerable, and *things a player might ask about* is not --- so a type-term answer needs a fallback rather than a table. **Deliberately separate from AB-42, and must not be used to decide it:** both horns need this, since a collection entry needs a key exactly as much as a node needs an identity. **A cost shared by both options cannot discriminate between them**, and folding it into the representation row would make the cheaper-looking option look cheaper than it is. **Split 2026-08-09 by [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), and half of it dissolves.** A **blanket** licence limit is keyed by **the entity it attaches to** --- an id that exists --- so the hard version of this question never arises there; the row's original framing was a category error, since a licence limit is keyed by its *holder*, not by what it denies. **What remains is the narrowing case, and it is better-posed than it was:** not *give identity to a nonentity*, which cannot be done, but *describe a closed region of licence space*, which is an ordinary predicate/description problem. Comparably hard, but no longer ill-formed --- and the closed-vocabulary trap below applies to it unchanged. | AB-21, AB-42, AB-18, AB-38, P5 | **Open for narrowing licences only --- the blanket case is dissolved by P5; question reshaped from identity to description** |
| **AB-44** | **Do all negatives carry causal force, or only some?** Opened 2026-08-09, and it is plausibly **upstream of AB-42 rather than beside it.** *There is no deep fryer* looks **inert**: nothing downstream reasons from it, and it exists only to keep the world's answers stable across repeated asking. *There is no battery* is **load-bearing**: a consequence follows from it and propagates. **This is [AB-19](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s description/mechanism split arriving in a place it was not written for**, after [C16](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c16-the-bare-table-and-the-room-that-is-just-a-mess) already used it to decide the messy room --- the plan has taken repeated arrival as weak evidence of a real joint before, and should here too. **If it holds, negatives are not one kind and AB-42 may have a per-kind answer rather than a global one:** a mechanism-negative earns a node because something depends on it; a description-negative may not, which would also bound AB-42's accumulation cost to the smaller set. **It also structures lifecycle, which is otherwise homeless.** An inert negative is a candidate for expiry --- [PS-10](#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s *how does a lead die on purpose* --- while a load-bearing one **cannot** silently vanish without the cascade depending on it going wrong. **Do not settle decay for negatives before this row.** **One warning against the easy reading, on AB-19's own precedent:** *load-bearing* must not become a compile-fictional-physics judgment computed from structure. AB-19 already rejected deciding consequence deterministically rather than by an LLM step. What this row asks is whether the **representation** differs by kind --- not whether the system can decide which negatives matter. | AB-19, AB-42, AB-9, PS-10 | **Open --- newly opened; likely upstream of AB-42, and the lifecycle question is parked inside it rather than given its own row.** **Widened 2026-08-09 by [C18](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c18-three-place-settings-and-only-two-of-them-may-have-a-napkin): the inert/load-bearing split does not cover *licence* negatives (AB-45), which are neither.** **Then narrowed the same day by [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), which is a better outcome than the widening:** if every negative is a recorded licence narrowing, this row is no longer asking *which negatives are one thing versus another* but the sharper **does closing licence for X also assert that X is absent?** Licence-closure is then **universal** and causal force **variable** --- the split survives, on a smaller and better-defined surface. **The authoring exception is what keeps the two from simply collapsing:** an author may add a deep fryer to a kitchen whose licence is closed, so closure cannot entail absence in general | 
| **AB-45** | **What licenses improvisation inside a whole --- and how is the absence of that licence recorded?** Opened 2026-08-09 from [C18](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c18-three-place-settings-and-only-two-of-them-may-have-a-napkin). **The case, in one line:** three `PlaceSetting` wholes --- authored, minted from a coarse envelope, and recognized over five existing objects --- are **structurally identical**, and the first two may improvise a napkin while the third may not. **So licence is not derivable from the graph and must be recorded.** **The discriminator, sharper than provenance:** a whole with a coarse **intensional** description (*a place setting*, *what a tavern needs*) has open constituents; a whole that is **extensional** --- constituted by enumerating members, its name applied afterward --- licenses nothing further. **Improvisation gap 2 already states this** while describing a shortfall: *"its body is nearly empty and its member set is the content."* **What the record must not say, and this is the row's main trap:** *there is nothing else on this table* is a **completeness claim about the fiction**, and asserting it establishes a negative nobody established --- C17's closed-world defect one scale down, on the construct built to prevent it. The correct content denies the **whole's generative capacity**, not the napkin's existence: the napkin stays *not established* and remains improvisable by any other licensed route. **Consequently "blanket negative" is probably the wrong name** --- it is a licence statement, and calling it a negative invites the completeness reading --- **and naming it is part of this row.** **Not covered by the negatives cluster:** AB-43 has no referent to key (it denies an open class), AB-44's inert/load-bearing split contains neither option (it has no in-world causal consequence and is not inert --- it constrains the *system*), and AB-42 may answer it as a **property of the root node** rather than a node, which is a second independent route to that row's per-kind answer. **One collision to resolve rather than inherit:** AB-14 holds that *"`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, so provenance is invisible downstream."* **Licence is provenance-determined, so provenance cannot be fully invisible** --- this is the second dent in that claim after [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-7-the-negative-record-is-where-authored-and-improvised-truth-actually-collide-and-the-merge-stack-cannot-arbitrate-it) Finding 7, and the two should be settled together. **The intensional reading may soften it:** what must survive downstream is arguably *does this whole have an intensional description*, which is a property of the whole rather than a provenance tag --- if so the merge-layer claim holds and only the vocabulary needs widening. **Not asserted; it is the row's leading candidate.** **Reframed the same day, and the row is now secondary to [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item).** Treating licence as a **first-class graph item** rather than reasoning about it in the abstract resolves three of this row's four difficulties outright --- see P5's repair table --- and **inverts the framing recorded above**: licence is the primary construct and negatives are the shadow it casts, rather than licence being an odd third kind of negative. **The provenance collision is closed by it** (licence is carried by the whole; provenance only sets its initial value), and **the naming question is answered in substance**: *blanket negative* is the wrong name because it is a licence narrowed to zero. **What survives as this row's own work:** the exact vocabulary, and whether *licence closed for X* and *X is established absent* are one fact or two --- see P5's second open item. | AB-9, AB-14, AB-19, AB-29, AB-42, AB-43, AB-44, **P5** | **Open --- three of four difficulties resolved by P5; vocabulary and the closure-versus-absence identity remain** |
| **AB-47** | **Are flat genre-forbids the same construct as fact-derived permission narrowing, or a different kind?** Opened 2026-08-09 by [C21](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c21-two-ways-to-say-there-are-no-firearms). *"A fantasy setting in which firearms have not been invented"* and *"a fantasy setting, without firearms"* exclude identically and behave differently on four probes --- the first is **defeasible in-fiction** (a player who researches gunpowder should succeed; that is a story), licenses near-misses, and answers *why*; the second does none of these. **The candidate framing, and it is what makes this a difference in kind rather than degree:** a bare genre-forbid is closer to a **social contract** --- an out-of-character agreement about what story is being told, in the family of lines-and-veils --- than to a fictional contribution. **Four consequences none of which the causal form shares:** it is **inviolable rather than weighted**, making it the first construct in this design that is **not a matter of degree** (which bounds **AB-46**'s reach); breaching it is **not a narrative-integrity fault** but the system doing what it was told not to do, a different failure mode with a different repair; it has **no diegetic explanation and none should be invented**; and it constrains **narration**, not only world-state --- [P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item)'s licence governs what may be *minted*, this governs what may be *said*. **The unification argument, recorded because it is real and economical:** a social contract is a licence narrowed at the fractal's **root** with weight infinite and defeasibility none, and since no level is privileged a root licence is just a licence --- which absorbs three of the four consequences. **The fourth does not fit**, and the stronger objection is separate: **type separation makes the category error unrepresentable rather than forbidden.** If genre-forbids share a structure with world facts, something will eventually narrate one, improvise a reason for it, or reincorporate it as a contribution. **Weight-infinity is a rule that they must not be confused; a type distinction is an invariant** --- [C18 Finding 4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-4-the-scope-trap-and-why-it-is-structural-rather-than-a-rule-to-remember)'s move on its fourth arrival. **Settled 2026-08-09 after two reversals in one day: TWO constructs, sharing ONE enforcement point.** The movement is recorded rather than smoothed --- each turn supplied a *new* consideration rather than re-arguing an old one, which is what a balanced question looks like while it settles. **The argument that settles it is stronger than the one it was first opened on.** Draft 1 argued that type separation keeps a category error unrepresentable, **which depends on someone eventually erring**. The settling argument holds **under perfect discipline**: unify the two as *a licence narrowing with weight infinite*, and **six consumers each become a branch on whether the weight is infinite** --- the mint gate (is this overturnable by play at all?), the out-of-character response text, the escalation channel, reincorporation weighting, write authority, and the moderation signal. **Six for six, sharing only a storage shape. A sentinel that changes behaviour at every read site is not a unification but two constructs with extra steps** --- and the compromise (*one construct with a discriminating field*) collapses the same way, since a field determining every behaviour is a type distinction spelled as an enum. **The two differ concretely, not abstractly:** a genre boundary is not overturnable by any in-fiction effort and its response is *persuade other **players**, outside the fiction*, with an out-of-character channel to moderators; a weighted world fact is overturnable by effort proportional to its weight and its response is *nobody has ever done this --- it is **possible**, but a significant and long-term quest*. **Two consequences beyond the answer: AB-46 gains concrete semantics (weight is the effort required to overturn**, symmetric with *how much investment stands behind it*), and **the legibility caution below is answered rather than carried**, since both constructs now have a legible out-of-character response and the weighted one converts a refusal into a **quest hook**. ~~**Lean: two constructs.**~~ ~~**Reversed 2026-08-09** --- both arguments for two fell to one observation: a *mint-time* gate is sufficient.~~ **Draft 2's error, recorded because its shape recurs: the mint-gate finding removed *one* of two objections, and that was treated as making the case. An objection falling is not a conclusion arriving.** **What survives from it is the durable half --- one enforcement point:** **You cannot stop a player assembling a tube, powder and shot and asking for a gun --- but you can refuse to mint the abstraction, and then the system steadily refuses to reason about the assembly.** **That answers the narration objection by re-scoping rather than rebutting it:** the gate catches the **high-blast-radius** case (a minted gun, interacted with and load-bearing three sessions later) and lets through the low one (a stray NPC line about gunners) --- which is [the integrity->prevention / coherence->damping split](#the-feedback-regime-is-the-thing-to-measure-and-it-decides-which-solutions-to-look-for-2026-08-09) applied correctly, not a leak tolerated. **And the refusal is structurally silent rather than obeyed** --- an unminted abstraction produces no packet, so there is nothing for a reasoning step to comply with. **The unrepresentability argument shrank rather than fell:** of its three legs, *narrate* goes with the above, *explain* reduces to a **discriminating field** (does this narrowing carry a causal account?), and only *reincorporate* still wants weight-infinity --- from *needs a type* to *needs a field*. **What survives is a different distinction than the one argued: write authority.** A world fact changes by authoring or play; a social contract changes by **out-of-character table negotiation** --- plausibly a fourth provenance beside authored / player-driven / system-improvised, separating from *authored* only in multiplayer. ~~**Revised answer: one construct, a discriminating field, a distinct write authority.**~~ **Superseded --- see the settled answer at the head of this row.** **One caution carried forward, and one discharged.** *Carried:* blast-radius reduction **assumes a moderator eventually arrives**, which weakens as scale grows. *Discharged:* silent refusal being safe but not legible --- **answered by the two out-of-character responses above**, which are legible precisely *because* they are out-of-character, so nothing has to be explained in-world where there is no in-world reason. **One consequence deliberately left out of scope:** the gate yields **moderation instrumentation for free**, with graded *approach* detection falling out of near-miss behaviour --- see [C21](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-7-the-gate-produces-moderation-instrumentation-for-free-and-that-may-be-the-larger-prize) Finding 7. The **table's social contract and the moderation practice around it are not this initiative's** to design. | AB-19, AB-46, P5 clauses 2/8/9, PL-1 | **Settled 2026-08-09 --- two constructs, one enforcement point.** Residual work is the write-authority question (table negotiation as a possible fourth provenance) and the out-of-character response surface, not a representation fork |
| **AB-46** | **Narrative weight: how much investment stands behind an established fact, and where is it carried?** Opened 2026-08-09 from [Narrative integrity is in service to reincorporation](#narrative-integrity-is-in-service-to-reincorporation-2026-08-09). **The requirement:** if narrative integrity serves **reincorporation of participant contributions**, then what a contradiction costs depends on **who invested in the thing contradicted** --- authored, worked-for-in-play, or improvised in passing. A tavern flipping well-stocked to poorly-stocked is near-costless when the system improvised both states and **expensive** when an author wrote it that way or players hauled the supplies in. **Weight is therefore the dial the damping rule needs**: protect high-weight commitments, let low-weight ones drift. **Where it goes:** *alongside or within the permissive structures* --- a licence record carrying not only what was established but how much stands behind it. **Why it does not fight [P5](#licence-transfer-import-is-a-copy-and-provenance-is-deliberately-not-traced-2026-08-09)'s no-cascade decision, which is the first thing to check and it passes:** weight is a **value carried on the record**, not a pointer to its source. Copy, not reference --- the same discipline licence transfer already adopted, so recording weight requires no provenance graph and no cascade. **The raw material already exists in storage:** authored (participation stack / WML), player-driven (kernel-committed step sequences and their events), system-improvised (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION`). **This row is not "how do we know" but "what scale, carried where, and consumed by whom."** **Concrete semantics supplied 2026-08-09 by [C21](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-4-two-constructs-one-enforcement-point--settled-after-two-reversals-in-a-day): weight is the *effort required to overturn*.** Pleasingly symmetric with *how much investment stands behind it* --- the more that went in, the more it takes to undo --- and it gives the scale an operational reading rather than a comparative one. **It also converts refusals into hooks:** *nobody has ever done this; it is possible, but a significant and long-term quest* is a weight statement, and it is the response that distinguishes a weighted world fact from a genre boundary (**AB-47**), which no effort overturns. **Bound established by the same case:** a genre boundary is **not on this scale at all**, so weight is not the universal currency --- do not model it as one with an infinite value. **Open sub-questions:** is weight a scalar or a provenance tag; does it decay (a contribution from a year ago versus last session --- and note [PS-10](#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) asks a structurally similar question for parked impulses); does player-driven outrank authored or the reverse; and does weight belong only on licence records or on any established fact. **Deliberately not designed now** --- the requirement is recorded so it is not rediscovered, and the plan has no consumer able to act on it until damping is being built. | AB-14, AB-29, AB-45, AB-47, P5, PS-4, PS-10 | **Open --- requirement recorded, deliberately undesigned; compatibility with P5's no-cascade checked and holds; semantics narrowed 2026-08-09 to *effort to overturn*, and bounded away from genre boundaries by AB-47** |

### Constraint matrix

The discipline this plan exists to enforce: **these do not close independently.** A row's answer is only provisionally valid until every row it constrains has been re-checked against it.

```text
   AB-12  (is there a privileged level at all?)  ---- may dissolve ---->  AB-4
      |                                                                  AB-2
      v
   AB-1   (root: one concept or several?)
      |
      +-----------------+------------------+
      |                 |                  |
 STRUCTURE          LIFECYCLE          CONSUMPTION
 AB-2  role         AB-5  minting      AB-9   level / projection
 AB-3  root         AB-6  decay        AB-11  operand vs step
 AB-4  level-edge   AB-10 authored     AB-7   state
 AB-8  extent       AB-14 minting      AB-15  default binding
 AB-13 DAG shape          modes        AB-19  describe vs mechanism
 AB-16 part kinds   AB-17 reabsorption AB-22  annotation
                    AB-18 recog. scope        vocab / timing
                    AB-20 failure/auth AB-23  situational
                    AB-21 identity            meaning
                    AB-24 context tier

   NEGATIVES  (added 2026-08-09, from C17)

   AB-44  (do all negatives carry causal force?)
      |
      |  if the description/mechanism split holds, AB-42 is answered
      |  per kind rather than globally --- so this closes first
      v
   AB-42  (negatives: ludic-graph nodes, or a collection beside the graph?)
      |
      +---- pressures ----> AB-12   (a negative may be the first thing
      |                              that genuinely cannot be an Object)
      |
      +---- parks lifecycle/decay inside AB-44, with PS-10

   AB-43  (what is a negative keyed by?)   NOT a dependency of AB-42:
                                           both horns need it equally,
                                           so it cannot discriminate
```

**The block above maps AB-1--AB-24 and the new negatives cluster only.** Rows AB-25--AB-41 have never been drawn into it and are carried by the coupling table and by each row's own dependency column --- stated so a reader does not read the diagram as complete.

**Three scheduling tiers (AB-14, AB-18, AB-24), each parameterizing the one above it:**

| Tier | Immediacy | Job |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **On-demand mint** | Blocking --- a pending operation cannot proceed | The referent must ground *now* |
| **Recognition** | Deferred, but promotable on demand | Did an arrangement compose into something? |
| **Context shift** | Low, ambient | Should we be watching for *different kinds* of meaning? |

**AB-14 is the lifecycle cluster's spine.** One mechanism --- improvisation --- invoked in two modes divided by **criticality**: on-demand (a pending operation is blocked) and recognition (nothing is blocked). AB-20 draws the two consequences of that axis (failure semantics, authorization), AB-21 the identity obligation it implies, AB-18 recognition's scope and the convergence of its two invocation paths, AB-17 the inverse operation, and AB-19 whether recognition is even a mint or merely AB-9 by another name.

Cross-cluster couplings that make a one-thread-at-a-time approach fail:

| Coupling | Why it bites |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-9 -> AB-5, AB-6** | If summary is a projection, minting and decay largely evaporate --- there is nothing stored to mint or to let rot. Answering AB-5 first would design machinery AB-9 may delete. |
| **AB-6 -> AB-3, AB-4** | A *derived* member set needs no root and no stored level-crossing edge; a *stored* one needs both, plus a drift-repair path. The structure cluster cannot close before this. |
| **AB-8 -> AB-4** | Framing (c) makes extent a composition case outright. Since (a) is also cheaper than first assumed, AB-8's cost is dominated by AB-4's answer rather than by any storage obstacle --- deciding AB-4 without testing (c) risks building host-spanning machinery for nothing. |
| **AB-2 -> AB-11** | If an abstraction is a node, it is already an operand and the executor may need no new vocabulary. If it is only a host or only a projection, "pull the rope" has nothing to bind to. |
| **AB-7 -> AB-1** | If state turns out to belong to a different lane entirely, example 3 leaves this initiative and AB-1's answer changes shape --- possibly from three axes to two. |
| **AB-10 -> AB-6** | An authored pattern definition is exactly what makes a *derived* member set cheap (match the pattern) instead of expensive (recognize ad hoc). |
| **AB-12 -> AB-1, AB-2, AB-4** | If no level is privileged, composition needs no new type and AB-4 becomes "what edge relates an Object to an Object at another level," not "what is a level-crossing relation." AB-1 then arbitrates only extent vs. state. Answering the structure cluster before AB-12 risks designing a type the reframe deletes. |
| **AB-14 -> AB-9** | Both minting operations are write-side, so AB-9 keeps its scope control **only if** its no-creation guardrail holds. AB-19 is where that boundary is actually contested: recognition-for-description may be indistinguishable from a projection. |
| **AB-19 -> AB-22 -> AB-18** | Superseded chain (2026-08-05). The authored-pattern-vocabulary shortcut is rejected, so AB-18's search is **not** bounded by a pattern library. It is bounded instead by AB-22's annotation vocabulary: if members carry structural-role affinities computed at mint time, an inert arrangement is cheap to skip and a causally loaded one is cheap to notice. **Design AB-22 before AB-18's cost policy** --- annotation timing determines whether the search is expensive at all. |
| **AB-14 -> AB-16, AB-17** | The minting modes cut *across* direction: on-demand minting usually runs downward and recognition upward, but neither is required to, and AB-16/AB-17 describe the same territory from the structural side. If direction and criticality turn out to be independent axes, these rows stay separate; if they co-vary, several collapse. **Narrowed 2026-08-06:** AB-16 is retired, so this interaction is now AB-14 -> AB-17 only --- and AB-16's minting-license fragment landed *on* AB-14/AB-17 rather than collapsing them. |
| **AB-20 -> AB-18, AB-19** | Authorization is *why* only recognition needs a confidence gate. Designing AB-18's trust story without that grounding invites re-deriving it as arbitrary policy --- or, worse, imposing a symmetric confidence requirement on on-demand minting, which the player's own reference already licenses. |
| **AB-21 -> AB-18** | The convergence requirement (one mint, two invocation paths) and the identity obligation (one wall, many references) are the same problem asked from two directions. A `stableKey`-shaped answer to one is probably the answer to both --- **and `Meta::Result`'s `nonCollidingPutItem` first-write-wins already supplies the mechanism**, see the `thinking` section. |
| **AB-23 -> AB-22, AB-19** | Situational meaning makes mint-time annotation a partial answer at best, and makes the member-affinity *filter* context-dependent rather than fixed. Settling AB-22's timing and vocabulary before AB-23 would design a static annotation scheme the situational answer immediately invalidates. |
| **AB-24 -> AB-18** | Tier 3 parameterizes tier 2: what recognition looks for is set by the current interpretive frame. AB-18's scope and trigger cannot be specified against an unspecified vocabulary. |
| ~~**AB-16 -> AB-8, AB-11**~~ | ~~Ordered extent and unordered parts need different carriers and different operations.~~ **Void 2026-08-06 on two counts.** The ordering premise was already wrong (**AB-26**: the structure is an adjacency graph, not a path), and AB-16 is now retired --- so neither "different carriers" nor "different operations" has a difference left to rest on. `pull` and `clear the place setting` differ as *operations*, which is the separability question now sitting on the manipulation ladder. |
| **AB-17 -> AB-5** | A minting design with no reabsorption criterion is a ratchet. The two must be designed together or the second will be retrofitted onto a shape that cannot express it. |
| **AB-13 -> AB-3** | Multiple parents mean a node sits in several closures at once, which is the doubly-reachable case `rootId`-is-recorded-not-derived already exists to handle. Whether that guard generalizes or merely rhymes is a real question. |
| **AB-44 -> AB-42** | If AB-19's description/mechanism split holds for negatives, they are **not one kind**, and the representation question gets a per-kind answer rather than a global one --- a mechanism-negative earns a node because a cascade depends on it, a description-negative may not. Deciding AB-42 globally first would either give every inert *there is no X* a node (paying AB-42's accumulation cost across the largest possible set) or deny one to a negative something genuinely propagates from. **AB-44 also bounds the cost that is AB-42's main argument against nodes**, so closing them in the wrong order prices the fork wrong. |
| **AB-42 -> AB-12** | A negative has no `shortName`, no body and no placement, so nodes-for-negatives needs a third node tag beside `'Character' \| 'Object'`. **That pressures AB-12's everything-is-an-Object reframe rather than being absorbed by it** --- possibly the first thing this initiative has found that genuinely cannot be an Object. Note the direction: AB-12 has until now only ever *dissolved* rows (AB-4, AB-2, composition); this is the first row that pushes **back** on it. |
| **AB-42 -> P2, P4, H3 clause 5** | Not AB rows, so recorded here rather than in the diagram. All three supply the same argument from different directions: a collection holding facts that cannot be edge endpoints is [P2's phantom](#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge) one scale down; P4's dataflow cascade propagates **between nodes**, so a non-node negative forces a second structure onto the hot path; and H3 clause 5's ports need an interior end to bind, which a collection entry does not have. **If AB-42 resolves toward the collection, all three need re-checking rather than assuming they tolerate it.** |
| **AB-43 -\|- AB-42** | **Recorded as an explicit *non*-coupling**, because the natural assumption runs the other way. Keying a negative whose referent does not exist is hard, and it is **equally** hard for both horns --- a collection entry needs a key exactly as much as a node needs an identity. **A cost shared by both options cannot discriminate between them**, and letting AB-43's difficulty attach to only one horn would make the collection look cheaper than it is. Close them independently. |

**A pattern worth promoting rather than treating as three coincidences.** AB-15 has now surfaced three times in the same shape --- **authoring supplies a default, play revises it**: the default *binding level* for unqualified reference (AB-15), the default *pattern vocabulary* for what can be recognized (AB-19), and the default *interpretive frame* a context-shift task detects deviation from (AB-24). If that generalizes, it is a single principle rather than three separate authoring questions, and it makes each of the three cheaper: the expensive open-ended inference becomes deviation-detection against an authored baseline.
---

Cases land in [Phase 0 corpus](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#phase-0-corpus).

Traced against the shipped code rather than assumed: `computeCarryClosure` walks only the **room's** `relationalEdges`; an already-decomposed member's interior structure lives in **its own** `positionGraph`, a different graph the walk never reaches. Absorption only ever attaches a member by its **root id** --- which is exactly [H3 clause 4](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s grounding-invariance guarantee (outside a whole, only the root/port is visible) composing with [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s clause-2 finding (whole and part are roles, not kinds, at any depth). **Not a new finding --- confirmation that two already-established clauses compose correctly**, which is why no new corpus case was needed to close it. Even the one place it seemed like it might bite (an *interior* part of the decomposed member tied to something back in the room) already has an answer: P3's original rope-tied-to-a-rock scheme, where an interior part's exterior tie is represented via a port on the whole, never by reaching through it.

A microphone in one room and a speaker in another, spliced together by wire the player carries between them --- an above-scale "rudimentary intercom" that is recognised, not authored, closing the gap [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system) and [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable) both left open by being authored themselves. **Satisfies the letter of the bullet without answering AB-14:** today's model has no mechanism to run beat (4) at all (the objects lane has no concept of a component whose identity is a set of other components), so this is the first case those rows can argue from, not a resolution of them.

  - [ ] **(F) The tightly-authored closed world --- P5's first falsification case, re-aimed 2026-08-09 and still unwritten.** An author builds a mystery, or any setting whose point is that **its inventory is exactly what was written**: no unlisted rooms off the corridor, no extra servants, no convenient tool in the drawer. **The author wants silence to mean *no*, and [clause 1](#the-scheme-as-a-clause-table-numbered-2026-08-09) says silence means *maybe*.** **Targets clause 1 directly, and clause 1 is the most load-bearing thing P5 has** --- unbounded-by-default is what lets an infinite detail space go unrepresented, so if it has a legitimate opposite the proposal's foundation is in question rather than one of its consequences. **The obvious repair --- zero the licence at the root and widen selectively below --- forces *composition semantics*, which are unworked ([PL-1](#open-questions-p5-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s licence-shaped half).** So one case pressures the most load-bearing clause **and** the biggest open one. **No predicted repair is recorded beyond naming that fork**, per the correction below.

    **What it replaced, and why --- recorded because retiring a falsification case needs a reason, not a quiet substitution.** C20 was originally the **warm-but-poor tavern**: a tavern authored poor, containing a place setting, where improvisation inside must not produce silver and china. **It was retired 2026-08-09 on the grounds that its central contradiction was not P5's but this plan's own drafting error.** Clause 3 said a limit reaches *no further* than its holder **without saying in which direction**; containment scope is directional, so a place setting's limit reaching the tavern is outside its holder-scope while the tavern's reaching inward is inside it. **No contradiction, and C11's internal-contradiction mode was mis-claimed.** **Everything else the tavern case would have tested either relocates or survives elsewhere:** clause 10 was **resolved** rather than broken (permission copied at import, context read live), clause 9's *warm but poor* grounding is recorded on that clause directly, and the residue --- **which** of a container's facts reach inward --- is **entirely PL-1**, since *is the tavern out of napkins because the last one went onto that very place setting?* is a causal story an LLM reasons out, not a rule a structure enforces. **The tavern survives as a PL-1 illustration**, where it does real work, rather than as a falsification case, where it did none. ~~**A predicted repair, recorded now so the case can confirm or refute rather than discover it:** character-limits are containment-transitive, inventory-limits are local.~~ **Withdrawn 2026-08-09 before the case was written, and the reason is methodological rather than a change of mind about the prediction.** **A queued falsification case carrying a named expected answer is a case that will be written to its prediction** --- and this plan has twice recorded the cost of a claim that sat unexamined until it was treated as settled. **The prediction also has a strike against it that was not visible when it was made:** a fixed table of which fact kinds propagate is close to the compiled-vocabulary move **AB-19 rejected**.

**What replaces it is the question rather than an answer --- [PL-1](#open-questions-p5-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused):** *of all the positive and negative facts held by containing abstractions higher in the fractal, how do we reasonably determine which are **relevant** to improvisation at a local level?* **That frame is wider than the prediction in the way that matters** --- it spans **positive** facts too (a roaring fire should inform local improvisation without being a permission), so it does not presuppose that licence is the only channel by which ancestor context reaches a local mint. Seven candidate answers are listed under PL-1, deliberately unranked, with **copy-at-import** among them as the one mechanism already designed --- and already known to be partial, since it says nothing about entities that never imported.

It is the most **player-visible** of the four --- docs and an unwired repairer mislead readers, but this one refuses a command --- and reconciliation must also settle the *inconsistency* between it and the relational route's silent hostless treatment, which is a defect on its own terms whichever way multi-host resolves.

A row that quietly settles a PQ by leaning on the lock is the momentum failure the lock was written to prevent, not an economy.

Only the first earns `H1-dependent`. Tagging retroactively does not work --- the reasoning that shows which one it is exists while the answer is being written and is gone afterwards.

Every row that a later answer invalidated goes back to Open with a note on what invalidated it. **Expect at least one round trip** --- if there is none, the provisional answers were probably not specific enough to conflict.

This is the point at which the set is finally known, and the count is the measurement the tagging exists to produce: **small** means H1 was cheap and most of the design stands without it; **large** means it is load-bearing and deserves more scrutiny than a hypothesis has had. Either result is informative; an untagged or over-tagged set produces neither.

Check each parked item's own prerequisites, and note that the backtrack channel is a **sibling initiative** to schedule against, not to absorb.

Check the entry against what actually shipped before promoting it; a Target entry that graduates unexamined is how a durable doc acquires a claim nobody tested. If AB-3 resolved yes, this is also when `CarryClosureFragment`'s pinned note gets discharged --- in both of its locations.

## Relationship to the object-manipulation ladder

Separate initiatives, real interactions. Recorded so neither plan silently assumes the other's shape.

### The dependency is asymmetric: representation unblocks reasoning

**Framing, 2026-08-05.** The reasoning layer has for some time been parking work that could not be addressed *because the right data did not exist*. A representation initiative therefore unparks some of that as a matter of course --- not coincidence, but the shape of the dependency. The ladder's rungs consume the world model; when a rung was parked for want of model, this is the initiative that supplies it.

**The inversion is the more useful half: those parks are a *specification*, not just a beneficiary list.** A rung parked for want of Feature nodes is a requirement for AB-12, written from the reasoning side before this initiative existed. **Phase 0's corpus should mine them** rather than inventing every case from scratch --- they have the advantage of being grounded in situations someone actually hit.

**But it holds for several parks, not all**, and the split matters so nobody expects the whole backlog to fall out:

| Data-blocked --- this initiative plausibly unblocks | Blocked on something else --- will not move |
| --- | --- |
| Feature look-matcher dispatch; character-inclusive catalog population (CPG-5 remainder) --- **AB-12** | **BD-24** / iteration 6, location-disambiguating modifiers --- *demand*-blocked, "no concrete case currently demands it" |
| **BD-16 (3)**'s `multiPresent` all-Error treatment --- explicitly waiting on a `Custom`-relation defer resolving into genuine multi-host co-location | **C3** / `MultipleCommands` policy --- same posture; no confirmed real-world example remains |
| Coyote's genre-bounded reasoning context --- **AB-19**; bounded by genre because there is no assembly-level structure to bound it with | **BD-18** / backtrack channel --- *architecture*-blocked: addressable resume points, bounded retry, cost policy |
| Situational meaning and the unpopulated mark-state axis --- **AB-23** | Iteration 2's remaining fallback steps --- prompt and calibration work |

| Touchpoint | Direction | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Referent resolution** | Ladder -> here | If abstractions are referrable ("clear the place setting"), Identify/Grounding must produce them as candidates. The already-tracked [character-inclusive catalog population](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) gap is the same shape of problem, and the node-model guidance for that scan (additive union getter, **not** a collapsed typed surface) applies here too. |
| **Synthesize instruction language** | Both | AB-11. The executor's ordered step sequence is where an abstraction-aware operation would live. BD-27/29/30's phase-stratified worklist and shared expansion environment are the machinery a level-crossing expansion would extend. |
| **Carry closure** | Here -> ladder | AB-3. If the graph gains a root, `CarryClosureFragment` collapses into it, which changes types the ladder's Expansion work owns. |
| **Backtrack (iteration 4)** | Ladder -> here | If an LLM can propose an abstraction (AB-5), a wrong proposal needs the same structured-critique channel BD-18 designs, not a silent guess. |
| **Reposition narration** | Adjacent | [`AGENT.relationalNarration.planning.md`](../../AGENT.relationalNarration.planning.md)'s Phase 1 asks what a reposition op *is*. A composition change ("the place setting came apart") is plausibly a fourth narration family. Do not pre-empt it; note the overlap when Phase 5 picks a slice. |
| **Separability and merger** | **Here -> ladder (handed over 2026-08-06)** | The first thing this initiative has *given* the ladder rather than asked of it. Cutting a rope makes two ropes; pulling a battery does not make two flashlights --- a difference in what a *divide* operation yields, so it is manipulation vocabulary, not representation. Left over from the retired **AB-16** and recorded on the ladder's [Deferred, not rung-sized](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) list. **Gated on this initiative:** the fractal has to settle before it is clear *how* separation and merger can happen, and only then is there a planning process worth designing. **AB-31** (fission) is the row on this side that will produce the answer. |
| **Features** | Constraint | [`positionGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/positionGraph/AGENT.md#known-limitation-deferred) records that Features are deliberately **narrower** participants than Objects --- never a relation subject, never membership-moved, more like a room's walls than its contents. An abstraction design must not assume a Feature node would inherit Object capabilities. |

## Discussion record

**Moved 2026-08-07 to [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md), unchanged.** It is provenance --- *why* the rows are shaped the way they are --- and is not needed to know what to do next, which is exactly the profile that should not sit in the middle of a working plan. **It was moved rather than trimmed:** this plan records supersessions instead of erasing them, and compaction would violate that discipline.

New entries go in that file, newest last, in the same style.

## Verification

No code slice exists yet. Until Phase 6, verification is that the baseline still passes and that no durable doc has acquired normative text for unbuilt behavior:

```bash
cd lambda/ephemera && npm run test -- --watchAll=false \
  dataSource/positions/ \
  dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/
```

Documentation checks, from the repo root. **This plan is now three files**, so anchor checking must run across all of them and resolve cross-file targets:

```bash
git diff --stat -- lambda/ packages/          # expect only AGENT.concepts.md
git diff --name-only -- '*/AGENT.contract.md' # expect EMPTY until Phase 6
```

- Every `](#anchor)` resolves **within its own file**, and every `](Other.md#anchor)` resolves in that file. The three files cross-link heavily and a rename breaks links silently.
- `AGENT.contract.md` acquires nothing before Phase 6. The concepts entry is **Target**-marked and carries its exclusion list; a concepts edit that drops either is the anti-pattern arriving by the side door.

Phase 6 adds its own commands here.
